# SAIRN Active Work — Hank

Per-session active-work log for the **Hank** session (`Documents\SAIRN-hank`).
Split out of the shared `SAIRN-ACTIVE-WORK.md` on 2026-08-24 because four
concurrent sessions appending to one file produced repeated merge conflicts
in a single night. Each session now appends only to its own file, so two
sessions can never collide on the same lines.

**Append your entries here, not to `SAIRN-ACTIVE-WORK.md`.**

The four sessions and their files — one row each, all equal, no special case:

| Session | Clone | File |
|---|---|---|
| Hank | `Documents\SAIRN-hank` | `SAIRN-ACTIVE-WORK-hank.md` ← this file |
| CC | `Documents\SAIRN-cc` | `SAIRN-ACTIVE-WORK-cc.md` |
| Cody | `Documents\SAIRN-cody` | `SAIRN-ACTIVE-WORK-cody.md` |
| Fourth | `Documents\SAIRN-fourth` | `SAIRN-ACTIVE-WORK-fourth.md` |

Still read the other three (`SAIRN-ACTIVE-WORK-cc.md`, `SAIRN-ACTIVE-WORK-cody.md`, `SAIRN-ACTIVE-WORK-fourth.md`) before starting anything —
the point of the shared file was to avoid two sessions touching the same app
file at once, and that reason has not gone away. Splitting the file removes
the write collision, not the need to check.

One line per task: app/file(s) touched, a one-line task description,
timestamp. Add a line before starting anything new.

Entries below were moved verbatim from the shared file (nothing reworded,
nothing summarized), in their original order. Cross-session context that
predates the split — including the `cc-work` worktree correction — stays in
`SAIRN-ACTIVE-WORK.md`.

---

- sairnsenior.html + api/claude.js (Hank) -- SIXTH SAIRNsenior BATCH DONE, AI Ops Assistant agentic tool, pushed (`f52e7ec`). New get_ops_attention tool (Claude tool-calling, custom/client-executed per the platform's Anthropic-tools contract), following the same get_X_attention pattern already proven on SAIRNcash/SAIRNgrounds/SAIRNscape/SAIRNlegacy/SAIRNdesign -- one consolidated tool, SEN_TOOLS register/execute wrapper, thrown errors become honest {ok:false}, two-step tool_use/tool_result round trip. insufficient_hours compares real delivered hours (completed visits' clock_in/out) against each active client's Authorized Hours/Week over the most recently FULLY COMPLETED 7-day window, deliberately excluding the current in-progress week to avoid flagging a client "behind" on a week that isn't over -- scoped by the same sen_clients three-tier read gate as everywhere else (broad-read roles see the active roster, caregiver sees only their own assigned clients). denial_patterns groups denied claims by denial_reason with counts/dollar totals, management-only (matching Billing & Claims' own gate, returns null for caregivers), and withholds a breakdown below 2 real denied claims (DENIAL_PATTERN_MIN_CLAIMS) rather than call two data points a "pattern" -- same discipline as SAIRNcode's DENIAL_PATTERN_MIN_EVENTS. New shared visitHours(v) helper factors the clock_in/out arithmetic out of generateClaim() so a claim and an hours finding can never silently diverge on it. - sairncode.html + api/sd-data.js + sql/sairncode_coded_items_schema.sql (Hank) -- SAIRNCODE GAP-CLOSURE PHASE 1 DONE, pushed (`5fa7922`), live-verified. Items 1+2 built as ONE resource (sc_coded_items, 25th SC_RESOURCES entry) rather than two, because they describe one object -- a coded item carrying its citation, reasoning, and review state; splitting them would have been speculative structure. Rides the existing generic sc_* handler, so the server change is three registration points only, no new branch. ITEM 1 (citation/reasoning as REAL STORED DATA): the honest finding first -- suggestCodesFromNote() was ALREADY doing the hard part (requiring an exact quote from the note, then independently verifying that phrase is genuinely a substring of the pasted text, a real check not a model claim) and then THREW ALL OF IT AWAY. Confirmed by grep across the function's whole body before building: zero scData/localStorage writes, render-only. So a coder could see a verified citation on screen and never reproduce it afterwards -- backwards for a compliance tool where "why was this code assigned" is the question that matters at audit time. Now each captured suggestion stores code/type/encounter ref/quote/quote_verified/reasoning/source_rule/who/when, and the capture path reads the suggestion out of a held array rather than the DOM so the stored quote is byte-identical to the one the verification actually ran against. ITEM 2 (honest escalation, never a forced best guess): confidence is a DERIVED label ('high'/'low') computed by scDeriveCodedItemConfidence() from signals this app can genuinely check -- no citation, citation not confirmed present in the note, no supporting rule, no code -- and any of them forces review_status='needs_human_review' with a plain-language reason. THE LOAD-BEARING DECISION, grounded in this file's own history rather than preference: no model is ever asked for a confidence percentage, and no numeric score exists anywhere in the schema. sairncode.html:2454 carries a standing comment recording that the Fraud panel used to seed fabricated 82%/71% confidence scores until the 2026-08-18 fabrication audit removed them; a percentage on a CODE ASSIGNMENT would be the same bug with higher stakes, because a number like that reads as a measured accuracy rate to whoever sees it next. Nothing promotes a flagged item automatically -- it waits for a real human decision recorded with employee id and timestamp. THREE REAL BUGS CAUGHT DURING THE BUILD, none shipped: (1) I referenced a `scSession` global that DOES NOT EXIST in this file -- the employee id lives in localStorage('sc_employee_id'); found by checking rather than assuming, fixed with a single scCurrentEmployeeId() read path. (2) THE IMPORTANT ONE -- the verification gate was written `quote_verified === false`, which FAILS OPEN: an item whose verification result was missing or unknown got treated as verified and auto-assigned on no grounds, the exact outcome this whole feature exists to prevent. Now `!== true`, failing closed. Caught by an isolated-logic test, NOT by reading the code -- the test that exposed it was specifically probing the undefined case. (3) Used a `table-wrap` CSS class that does not exist in this file (grep returned 1 hit, my own); corrected to the real table-container/tw pattern every other panel uses. VERIFICATION: node --check clean on both script blocks and api/sd-data.js; div balance 788/788; 24 nav targets / 24 panels 1:1; zero duplicate ids; zero duplicate function names; dead-button audit 75 handlers / 0 missing. Escalation logic isolated-logic tested 12/12 against the REAL function extracted verbatim from the file (not a retype), including three fail-closed cases (undefined, truthy string, numeric 1) and an explicit assertion that no numeric confidence field exists in the output shape. LIVE-VERIFIED after push: polled the real deployed sairncode.html until markers appeared -- all 10 present including the `quote_verified !== true` fail-closed guard in the deployed source, and zero numeric-confidence matches; sc_coded_items now passes the live resource allowlist returning an honest {"ok":true,"data":[],"provisioned":false} (migration queued, not run), with a bogus resource name still 400ing as the contrast control. First rebase attempt went wrong in two ways I caught and fixed before anything reached origin -- a Python rewrite stripped every CRLF from api/sd-data.js (core.autocrlf=true normalizes on add, but the repo's blob for that file is CRLF), turning a 3-line change into a 6147-line whole-file rewrite; and restoring from a MOVING origin/main absorbed CC's alf_staff work into my commit. Both fixed by rebasing onto CC's actual commit and re-applying only my own three additions with CRLF preserved via `git -c core.autocrlf=false add`. Final commit is 19 lines in sd-data.js, verified by diff to contain nothing but my additions, and CC's alf_clients/alf_staff confirmed intact both in-file and live (alf_staff returns a real NO_SESSION 401 post-deploy, not a regression). Unblocking it is Michael connecting a real Stedi API key in Settings (free account, 270/271 billed at $0 by Stedi, but it must be the practice's own account) -- not mine to do. ITEM 3 BUILT, two parts. (1) EXCEPTION-BASED ROUTING: the Coded Items panel now genuinely splits into an "Exceptions -- need a human coder" table (the real work queue) and an "Auto-assigned -- no action needed" table. The separation IS the feature -- the stated goal is that high-confidence codes stop consuming a coder's attention, which a single mixed list with a status badge does not actually achieve. Items a human has already decided on leave the queue in either direction (accepted or rejected), so the queue only ever holds genuinely undecided work. (2) TWO ADDITIONAL REAL SIGNALS, chosen specifically because both derive ONLY from data this practice itself created, so neither asserts any coding fact this app invented: (a) conflict with a rule the practice ITSELF verified -- reuses the existing findLocalScrubMatch() against sc_scrubrules, a table where every row required a human-entered Source and which is never seeded; a hit is deterministic and practice-verified, scoped to the same encounter_ref, and PROVEN BY TEST to stay silent when that table is empty, so an empty reference table can never manufacture a conflict; (b) a coder previously REJECTED this exact code -- pure history from this app's own recorded review decisions, and deliberately narrow: a prior ACCEPTANCE does not hold an item, and a merely-pending twin does not either, both tested. ON "CONFIDENCE-SCORED", the phase title says "scored" and this deliberately still produces NO numeric score anywhere -- the reasoning is now written into the code rather than left implicit, because that comment is what stops a future session from "fixing" its absence. A percentage implies a measured accuracy rate and nothing in this app measures one: no labelled outcome set, no base rate, nothing to calibrate against. This file already had fabricated 82%/71% confidence numbers removed from the Fraud panel in the 2026-08-18 audit; a number on a CODE ASSIGNMENT would be the same bug with higher stakes. What is real is which concrete checks passed or failed, so that is what is stored and displayed. BACKWARD-COMPATIBILITY DETAIL THAT MATTERED: scDeriveCodedItemConfidence gained an OPTIONAL context argument -- called without it, Phase 1 behaviour is byte-identical, so the four grounding checks never silently depend on context being available; 5 explicit Phase 1 regression tests cover this. - sairncode.html + api/sc-ai.js (Hank) -- SAIRNCODE GAP-CLOSURE PHASE 3 DONE, pushed (`d51d352`), live-verified. ITEM 5 BUILT AS A RISK CHECK, NOT A PROBABILITY SCORE -- the data gate was checked live with a real admin session BEFORE writing any code, exactly as instructed, and it failed: sc_denial_events 0 rows, sc_denial 0, sc_ar 0, sc_prebill 0, and sc_claims held exactly 1 row which is the known CLM-MIGRATION-TEST residue (TestPayer, $1, status Pending -- a test artifact, not real and not even denied). There is no labelled outcome set, no base rate, and nothing to calibrate a predicted probability against, so no probability is produced anywhere. THE DISTINCTION THE WHOLE FEATURE IS BUILT ON, written into the code comments so it survives a future session that might try to "add the missing percentage": "34% chance this claim is denied" is a PREDICTION requiring a calibrated model that does not exist here and is never produced; "this payer denied this code 3 of your 12 logged denials" is a COUNT of events the practice itself recorded, which is real, checkable, genuinely actionable, and is what got built. Three risk factors, every one from the practice's own data: (a) codes on this encounter still sitting unresolved in the Phase 1/2 human-review queue; (b) a conflict between the claim's OWN codes per a rule THIS PRACTICE verified, reusing findLocalScrubMatch against the Source-required never-seeded sc_scrubrules table, and proven by test to stay silent when that table is empty so an empty reference can never manufacture a risk factor; (c) prior denials involving this payer plus a code on this claim, gated on the file's EXISTING DENIAL_PATTERN_MIN_EVENTS=3 rather than a second invented threshold -- below it the UI shows an honest "not enough logged denial history for a real rate (have N, need 3+)" and NO rate at all, rather than a softened or approximated number. LINKAGE DECISION MADE WITHOUT A SCHEMA CHANGE: claim records have no codes field, and rather than migrate that, the check uses the linkage that already exists -- a coded item's "Claim / encounter reference" matching the claim id. When nothing is linked, the UI says so plainly instead of appearing to have checked codes it never had. ITEM 6 BUILT, DRAFT-AND-HOLD, with two things stated SEPARATELY because conflating them would overclaim: (1) this app has NO send path of any kind -- no SMTP, no clearinghouse submit, no fax, verified by grep across sairncode.html before building (the only mailto: in the file is the trial-upgrade link) -- so "never auto-sends" is a property of the APPLICATION, not a safeguard this feature invented, and it is deliberately not claimed as one in either the code or the UI copy; (2) what this DOES genuinely add is grounding -- the draft is built strictly from the recorded denial row, and the prompt forbids inventing chart contents, dates, provider names, patient identifiers, or policy citations, requiring an explicit [BRACKETED] placeholder instead, and separately bars the model from asserting that documentation supports medical necessity since it has not seen the chart. Rides the existing audited/screened scAiFetch transport via a new appeal_draft KNOWN_FEATURES entry, inheriting the consent gate and injection screening automatically rather than opening a bypass. Isolated-logic tested 18/18 against the real functions extracted VERBATIM, covering the below-threshold honest-empty path, an assertion that no probability/score/percent key EVER appears in the output shape, real payer scoping (a different payer correctly yields 0 matches), per-encounter isolation of unreviewed codes, the empty-rule-table anti-fabrication case, and single-code self-pair exclusion. LIVE-VERIFIED after push: all 8 markers present in the real deployed sairncode.html with zero numeric-probability leaks; and the appeal_draft feature exercised END TO END against production with a real admin session -- returned audited:true (server confirms the audit-log write succeeded, not merely attempted) and a real 2,113-character draft that correctly bracketed [DATE OF SERVICE], a fact deliberately NOT supplied. Ran explicit anti-fabrication checks on that real output: zero invented regulatory citations (no 42/45 CFR, no CMS-nnnn, no Pub 100, no section numbers), zero unsupported "the documentation supports/establishes..." assertions, zero invented provider or patient identifiers, and 20 placeholders left for the human. The grounding discipline is working against the real endpoint, not just asserted in the prompt. PHASES 4-5 STANDING RULE RESTATED, now imminent: every specific fact in those phases (dollar thresholds, code ranges, modifier percentages, the CMS-vs-CPT 99292 conflict, license-gated code restrictions) came from this session's web research rather than primary sources, and each must be verified against an authoritative source (CMS.gov, AMA CPT, official Medicare manuals) BEFORE entering the tool, flagged per-item, with any error found reported plainly as the verification step working rather than quietly corrected. 2026-08-20
- sairncode.html + api/sd-data.js + sql/sairncode_credential_scope_schema.sql (Hank) -- SAIRNCODE PHASE 4 DONE, pushed (`51eb15a` item 7, `a729878` item 8), live-verified. ITEM 7, CREDENTIAL-GATING LAYER. Reported the gap BEFORE building as instructed, and it was structural, not just emptiness: sc_providers is empty (0 rows, verified live) AND could not have supported this gate even if populated -- its `cred` field is a Yes/No dropdown (credentialed-at-all, not per-code), its `specialty` is FREE TEXT so "Cardiology"/"cardiology"/"Cards" are three different values nothing can match a code against, and no claim, coded item, or denial record linked to a provider at all. So item 7 split into two honestly-separable halves: the validation layer plus the structured data it needs (built), and any claim about WHICH specialty may bill WHICH code (deliberately NOT built and never seeded -- that varies by payer policy and state scope-of-practice law and was not verified against a primary source; the practice enters scopes THEY verified, source required, same discipline as sc_scrubrules). New sc_credential_scope resource, 26th SC_RESOURCES entry -- a credential grant is genuinely a different object from a provider (one provider holds many, each with its own scope and source), same reasoning that kept sc_auth_requests out of sc_auth. THE GATE FAILS CLOSED, decision confirmed with Michael after I raised it rather than picking silently: no credential data returns 'no_data' and routes the coded item to human review rather than passing it, because an empty table silently green-lighting every code would be worse than having no gate -- identical principle to the quote_verified fail-open bug caught in Phase 1. It is a disclosed SOFT block: the message tells the coder to add the data or accept the code on their own recorded judgment, so an empty table never freezes the workflow. Reusable contract for every Phase 5 module: scValidateCredentialForCode(providerId, code) -> {state:'covered'|'no_data'|'not_covered'|'no_provider'|'no_code', ok, detail}; callers branch on state, never parse detail; only 'covered' is ok. Scope matching is literal and documented -- exact, prefix ("992*"), or range ("99281-99285") -- nothing inferred. ALSO FIXED, a real Phase 1 gap found while wiring boot render: renderCodedItems() was only ever called from scSyncAllResources(), so on a page load the Coded Items panel stayed empty until a server sync landed. Tested 26/26 including every fail-closed state, that a bare "*" matches nothing rather than everything, and specifically that an alphanumeric HCPCS code (A0430) can never fall inside a numeric CPT range just because its digits sort between the endpoints, with a numeric control proving the range still works. ITEM 8, CRITICAL CARE TRANSPORT -- EVERY FACT VERIFIED AGAINST A PRIMARY SOURCE BEFORE IT ENTERED THE TOOL, per the standing rule, AND TWO REAL CORRECTIONS WERE FOUND. Both on the same axis and both would have caused real misbilling if built as briefed: (1) 99466/99467/99485/99486 are PEDIATRIC, 24 MONTHS OF AGE OR YOUNGER -- the brief described them as generic "physician face-to-face" and "physician supervisory" time-based codes, and using any of them for an adult or older child would be flatly wrong; this is the defining element of the code family, not a nuance. (2) All four require INTERFACILITY transport -- face-to-face time begins when the physician assumes primary responsibility at the REFERRING facility, so a scene response or in-hospital transfer does not qualify. Both are now hard gates that FAIL CLOSED: undocumented age or undocumented interfacility status blocks rather than passes. CONFIRMED AS BRIEFED, no change needed: A0430 fixed wing / A0431 rotary wing / A0435 fixed-wing mileage / A0436 rotary-wing mileage (CMS Medicare Claims Processing Manual Ch. 15, which also yielded a real bonus rule -- air mileage in whole loaded statute miles, air ambulance payable only to a hospital); ventilation-management bundling, 94002-94004 and 94660-94662 not separately reportable with E/M and only the E/M payable (CMS NCCI Policy Manual Ch. XI); and the No Surprises Act IDR distinction applying to AIR ambulance but NOT ground (CMS No Surprises Act) -- that last was the highest-stakes claim in the set and it checked out exactly as briefed. Michael chose Option 1 when I raised how the verified facts should land: every rule is real app logic AND displays its source inline in the UI, so an outside reviewer can independently check "99466 requires age <= 24 months, source AMA CPT descriptor" in seconds rather than trusting an opaque gate or hunting for where a hardcoded value came from -- same show-your-work pattern as Phase 1 citations and Phase 2 rule-conflict reasoning. A test asserts EVERY finding carries a non-empty source naming CMS/AMA/AAP/NCCI. The two billing streams are structurally separated and displayed separately (A-codes billed by the transport SUPPLIER, 994xx by the PHYSICIAN, often to different payers); mixing them on one claim blocks. DELIBERATELY ABSENT: the ground ALS/BLS/SCT A-code variants. The brief referenced them but only the four AIR codes were verified against CMS in this build, and an unverified ground code has no business in a billing tool -- their absence is stated in the panel rather than silently omitted, and a test asserts none leaked in. VERIFIED: node --check clean on both script blocks and api/sd-data.js; div balance 839/839; 25 nav / 25 panels 1:1; zero duplicate ids; dead-button audit 81 handlers / 0 missing. Isolated-logic tested 27/27 on the transport module extracted verbatim -- both age boundaries (24 exactly = inside, 25 = outside), both fail-closed paths, stream separation, vent bundling, wing-type mismatch between transport and mileage, time-vs-descriptor checks, and that NSA is NEVER asserted for a non-air claim. LIVE-VERIFIED after push: all markers present in the real deployed sairncode.html including the pediatric age language and the NCCI citation, zero unverified ground codes in the deployed source, sc_credential_scope passing the live resource allowlist with an honest provisioned:false, and CC's newest alf_billing work confirmed unaffected. MERGE NOTE: CC pushed SAIRNcare three more times during this phase (MAR, Staff, Billing); the api/sd-data.js conflict was resolved with the now-established CRLF-safe procedure (rebuild from origin's bytes, re-apply only my own additions, `git -c core.autocrlf=false add`) giving an 18-line diff with CC's 34 alf_ references intact. TWO MIGRATIONS QUEUED, NOT RUN: sairncode_coded_items_schema.sql and sairncode_credential_scope_schema.sql -- both degrade honestly to local-only. 2026-08-20
- sairncode.html (Hank) -- SAIRNCODE PHASE 5 IN PROGRESS, 3 of 6 specialty modules done and live: item 9 Emergency Department (`9d6f8cd`), item 11 Physical Therapy (`bf1b274`), item 14 Mental Health (`62b639d`). All three rest on Phase 4 item 7's credential layer and reuse a shared scRenderRuleFindings() so "every rule shows its source" holds by construction rather than by repetition. FIVE REAL ERRORS FOUND AND CORRECTED ACROSS THE THREE, plus one case where my own doubt was WRONG -- reporting that one because it matters as much as the corrections. ITEM 9 (ED), THREE ERRORS: (1) THE BRIEF WAS TOO RESTRICTIVE, the first error in this arc running that direction -- it called for a HARD BLOCK on 99291 with an ED E/M same day, same provider, "real CMS violation otherwise." That is wrong: CMS PERMITS both when critical care follows a COMPLETED ED E/M, with modifier -25; only the reverse order is barred. A hard block would have rejected legitimate billing and cost the practice real revenue. Now order-dependent, and it fails closed when the sequence is undocumented. (2) 99217-99220 were DELETED 1 Jan 2023 (-> 99221-99223 initial, 99231-99233 subsequent, 99238/99239 discharge) -- but 99234-99236 in the SAME briefed claim REMAIN VALID, so only the wrong half was corrected and tests assert all three of 99234/99235/99236 are never flagged. (3) 99293-99296 were DELETED (-> 99471/99472/99468/99469) AND those replacements are INPATIENT-only, so the range was wrong twice over for an ED module; pediatric critical care in the ED uses 99291/99292 like adults. Confirmed as briefed: MDM-only leveling for 99281-99285, the critical-care clock PAUSING for separately billable procedures (31500, 36555/36556, 92950, 93010), and the real CMS-104-minute vs CPT-75-minute split for 99292, implemented as genuinely payer-dependent rather than picking one. ITEM 11 (PT), ONE ERROR + ONE REVERSAL: the CQ reduction is 15% of the FULL fee schedule amount (paid at 85%), NOT of the practice-expense component as briefed -- the PE component belongs to MPPR, a different reduction, and keeping the brief's wording would have mis-stated what a PTA-delivered unit actually pays. THE REVERSAL, reported plainly: I flagged the "new 2026 RTM codes 98979/98984/98985" as probably invented because they looked wrong against the established 98975-98981 family. THEY ARE REAL -- genuine 2026 additions for shorter 2-15 day monitoring windows, with the pre-existing device-supply codes now specifying 16-30 days. I nearly "corrected" a correct fact; the verification step cut both ways and that is exactly why it runs on everything rather than only on claims that look suspicious. It also caught an OMISSION -- 98986 (CBT, 2-15 days) is real and was missing from the brief, so it is included. Confirmed as briefed: the 8-minute rule aggregating the session FIRST then allocating (8-22=1, 23=2, 38=3, 53=4), the $2,480 CY2026 KX threshold, CQ required when a PTA furnishes MORE THAN 10%, and MPPR ranking -- refined to highest PRACTICE-EXPENSE RVU specifically. Two real nuances the brief omitted are now encoded: PT and SLP share a COMBINED KX threshold (OT has its own separate $2,480), and there is a second targeted-medical-review threshold at $3,000. The KX check also blocks when KX is applied with no recorded justification, since KX is an attestation and boilerplate defeats its entire purpose. ITEM 14 (MENTAL HEALTH), ONE ERROR + TWO FIGURES DELIBERATELY NOT ENCODED: MFTs/MHCs are paid at 75% of what a CLINICAL PSYCHOLOGIST is paid under the fee schedule, NOT "75% of the physician fee schedule" as briefed -- different numbers, and the brief's version would materially overstate expected reimbursement; the module states the correct basis AND explicitly denies the physician-rate reading, since that is the misreading most likely to persist. NOT ENCODED, per the fail-closed rule: the "~$42/session" POS 10 vs POS 02 swing (the DIRECTION is verified and enforced -- POS 10 pays the non-facility rate, POS 02 the facility rate, lower -- but the dollar amount is code- and locality-specific and could not be verified, so no figure is asserted, and a test asserts "$42" appears nowhere in the module); and a definition for H0020 (the other four H-codes were verified, H0020 was not, so entering it returns an honest "not verified by this tool, confirm against your own source" rather than a guessed description). Confirmed as briefed: LPC/LMFT/LMHC barred from 90792 and from the E/M-paired add-ons 90833/90836/90838, Medicare billing blocked until CMS-855I/PECOS enrollment completes, psychotherapy vs E/M time never double-counted, psychotherapy notes needing their own more restrictive tier per 45 CFR 164.501, telehealth keeping the same CPT with only POS changing, and family-therapy necessity anchoring on the patient's OWN diagnosis -- implemented as a real checkable pattern that rejects Z63.x relational codes as the anchor. The license blocks say plainly that they are LICENSURE limits, not documentation ones, because "write a better note" is the wrong and expensive response to them. VERIFIED across all three: node --check clean on both script blocks throughout; div balance 861/861 at the end; 28 nav / 28 panels 1:1; zero duplicate ids; dead-button audit 84 handlers / 0 missing. Isolated-logic tested 31/31 (ED), 40/40 (PT), 34/34 (MH) against the real modules extracted VERBATIM -- including the full published 8-minute table with every boundary, the 8+8+8=24 case where per-code counting says 3 units but the aggregate supports only 2 (the exact over-bill that rule exists to prevent), both 99292 payer boundaries (Medicare exactly 104, other payer exactly 75), the PTA exactly-10% boundary (CQ NOT required at 10, required at 11), and an assertion in every module that EVERY finding carries a non-empty source. LIVE-VERIFIED after each push. TWO LIVE-CHECK FALSE POSITIVES INVESTIGATED RATHER THAN REPORTED AS LEAKS: a grep for "$42" hit 2 times and one for a H0020 definition hit once -- both turned out to be my own grep patterns misfiring (the "$42" hits are inside the comment EXPLAINING that the figure is deliberately not asserted, and "H0020: true" matched a lowercase-letter pattern via the word "true"), confirmed by pulling the actual surrounding text before drawing any conclusion. MINOR, DISCLOSED: commit 62b639d's message contains a stray non-English word ("могли") from a typing glitch -- cosmetic, in the message only, not in any code; not force-pushed to fix, since rewriting shared-branch history while CC is actively pushing SAIRNcare would be far more disruptive than the typo. - sairncode.html (Hank) -- SAIRNCODE PHASE 5 COMPLETE, all 6 specialty modules built and live: item 9 Emergency Department (`9d6f8cd`), item 11 Physical Therapy (`bf1b274`), item 14 Mental Health (`62b639d`), and now items 10/12/13 together (`f48d780`) -- Cosmetic/Reconstructive, Optometry/Ophthalmology, Urgent Care. All 6 rest on Phase 4 item 7's credential-gating layer and share scRenderRuleFindings(), so "every rule shows its source" holds by construction. Verified live that all 8 validators (6 specialty + credential gate + transport) are deployed together. ITEM 10, ONE CORRECTION: the brief listed modifiers "59/51/76" together for MIXED same-session cosmetic/reconstructive cases. Modifier 76 is Repeat Procedure by the Same Physician -- the SAME procedure repeated -- and does NOT distinguish a cosmetic component from a reconstructive one (59 is for a DIFFERENT procedure, 51 for multiple procedures in one session). Appending 76 to a mixed session would be real misbilling, so it is BLOCKED with an explanation rather than offered, and a test asserts 76 is absent from the valid-modifier map while 59 and 51 are present. Also surfaced during that verification and included: CMS prefers the more specific X{EPSU} modifiers (XE/XP/XS/XU) over 59 where one applies, so those are accepted and a 59 use prompts that note. Cosmetic cases are hard-routed to the direct-pay workflow -- routing one into the insurance A/R pipeline blocks; reconstructive claims require BOTH documented medical necessity and objective evidence, and a prior related procedure in the patient's own claims history (e.g. a mastectomy) is recognised as real supporting evidence rather than ignored. ITEM 12, VERIFIED WITH NO CORRECTIONS: 92015 determination of refractive state separately billable from any 92xxx or 99xxx visit; -54 surgical care only / -55 postoperative management only, with transfer-of-care documentation required and, for -55 specifically, the post-op DATE RANGE; 66984 cataract carrying a 90-day global. ONE RULE ADDED that the brief never mentioned but which was confirmed and is genuinely useful: a refraction is NEVER part of a global surgical package, so 92015 stays separately billable even inside the 90-day cataract global -- that is exactly the kind of thing a coder loses money on by assuming the opposite. ITEM 13, VERIFIED WITH NO CORRECTIONS -- every briefed claim checked out, which is worth recording as plainly as the errors: S9083 "urgent care center global" and S9088, both appearing on CMS Outpatient CPT/HCPCS EXCLUDED Services lists consistent with Medicare not paying S-codes; standard E/M 99202-99215 for Medicare; POS 20. The ED-code block turned out to be MECHANICAL rather than a judgement call, because CMS defines POS 20 as "a location, DISTINCT FROM A HOSPITAL EMERGENCY ROOM" -- so 99281-99285 block unless hospital-based ED status is affirmatively recorded, and an unrecorded payer type also blocks since the same visit codes differently by payer. VERIFIED: node --check clean on both script blocks; div balance 875/875; 31 nav / 31 panels 1:1; zero duplicate ids; dead-button audit 87 handlers / 0 missing. Isolated-logic tested 43/43 across all three modules extracted VERBATIM, on top of the earlier 31/31 (ED), 40/40 (PT), 34/34 (MH), 27/27 (transport) and 26/26 (credential gate) -- 201 isolated-logic assertions across the whole Phase 4+5 arc, every one against real code pulled from the file rather than retyped. PHASE 5 ERROR TALLY, FINAL: six real errors found and corrected across items 8, 9, 10 and 14 (pediatric age limit and interfacility requirement on the 994xx transport codes; the over-restrictive ED co-billing hard block; deleted observation codes 99217-99220; deleted pediatric codes 99293-99296 that were also inpatient-only; the CQ practice-expense mis-attribution; the 75%-of-physician-rate error; and the modifier-76 mis-assignment). Three modules had NO errors at all (12, 13, and the confirmed half of 11). One case went the other way entirely -- the "new 2026 RTM codes" I suspected were invented turned out to be real, and I nearly corrected a correct fact. A NOTE ON PROCESS worth keeping: the errors were not concentrated in the claims that looked shakiest. The single most dangerous one (the ED hard block, which would have silently destroyed legitimate revenue) looked entirely safe, and the one I was most suspicious of was correct. That is the argument for verifying everything rather than triaging by apparent risk. TWO SHELL-QUOTING FAILURES hit while building this batch (heredocs breaking on embedded quotes) -- both caught because the command errored rather than silently half-applying, and the file state was checked before retrying rather than assumed; worked around by writing the insert script and the test files to disk via the Write tool instead of piping through the shell. 2026-08-21
- api/_resources/*.js (13 new files) + api/sd-data.js (Hank) -- STRUCTURAL FIX for the recurring api/sd-data.js merge collisions between parallel sessions, pushed (`d6f8afc`), live-verified with a before/after production diff. SCOPE DECIDED FROM EVIDENCE, NOT ASSUMPTION, and narrowed from the original request after raising it: Michael asked for one resource file per app with sd-data.js as a thin loader. Checked where the collisions ACTUALLY happen before designing anything -- all three conflicts hit while building SAIRNcode this session were in the shared RESOURCES object literal and the separately hand-maintained "resource must be one of" string, two shared lines every app appends to, while the request-handler branches changed by 100-189 lines in those SAME commits and merged cleanly EVERY time because each app appends in its own region. So the registry was split and the ~70 handler branches were deliberately left in place. Raised that as an explicit fork rather than silently narrowing scope; Michael chose registry-only. THREE FINDINGS THAT SHAPED IT: (1) there are ZERO duplicate resource+action branches, so dispatch is order-independent and no split can change which branch wins -- that is the fact that makes a future handler split safe if ever wanted, and it is worth recording because the file's own sairnscape comment warns about exactly that hazard for scp_jobs; (2) every branch is a closure over ~15 handler-local bindings (licHash, lic, isPaid, headers, rest, enc, nowISO, payload, action, resource, SUPABASE_URL, SERVICE_KEY, isScResource, req, res), so moving them means threading a context object through ~3,000 lines serving 11 live apps -- large behavioural risk for no additional collision benefit; (3) resources do NOT partition cleanly by app, which breaks the one-file-per-app framing: shared_knowledge is used by 10 apps, employees by 4 real apps, and schedule/invoices/jobs/customers are cross-app, so those live in _resources/shared.js rather than being forced into one app's file. TWO REAL PRE-EXISTING BUGS FIXED, both found during verification rather than looked for: (a) DRIFT -- the map held 158 resources but the hand-written error string listed 157; employee_profile is a valid, working resource that was MISSING from it, so a caller who mistyped got a "must be one of" list omitting a resource that actually works. The string is now GENERATED from the map, making that class of drift structurally impossible. (b) SILENT DUPLICATE REGISTRATION -- two apps claiming the same resource name previously just overwrote each other in an object literal with no signal at all; assertNoDuplicates() now throws at load, and that guard was live-tested by deliberately registering sc_denial under sairnbuild and confirming it throws with a message naming both apps. THE ONE DISCLOSED BEHAVIOUR CHANGE, raised with Michael before building rather than after: the single 400 diagnostic now lists 158 names grouped by app instead of 157 in the old hand-maintained order. Nothing was removed from it. No gate, no success path, and no resource's handling changed -- the only non-comment edits to sd-data.js are the require line and the error line. VERIFICATION, done the way everything else this session has been: node --check clean on sd-data.js and all 13 new files; the merged registry loaded in Node and compared against the ORIGINAL sd-data.js recovered from git rather than against my own notes -- 158 vs 158, set identical, every original key truthy in the new map, zero duplicates, nothing invented; the error-text delta computed exactly (ADDED employee_profile, REMOVED nothing); CRLF preserved throughout (3190 lines, 0 bare LF) using the established autocrlf-safe procedure; the generated per-app files produced by SCRIPT rather than hand-transcribed so 158 names could not be mistyped, with the original comment blocks kept attached to the resources they document (including the scp_ collision story). - sairnlaw.html + api/_lib/wex.js + api/_lib/intl-caselaw.js + api/legal-reference.js + sql/sairnlaw_wex_intl_schema.sql (Hank) -- SAIRNLAW PHASES A-D DONE, pushed (`1779346`, `ff26b05`, `e4fbb91`), live-verified. THE FIRST STEP CHANGED THE BUILD: Michael asked me to check whether Wex/BAILII/CanLII/AustLII have real APIs before writing code, and to consider a Bright Data connector rather than custom scraping. Checking each against its OWN primary terms found that TWO OF THE FOUR REFUSE THIS USE OUTRIGHT. BAILII prohibits bulk downloading and scraping in its standard user agreement and restricts crawlers, with its stated concern being AI software built to predict case outcomes -- it names this product category. AustLII goes further: its usage policy prohibits "spidering, scraping, crawling, mirroring, page framing, API access, bulk querying, automated agents" and blocks automated access for AI-related uses across its ENTIRE collection, with even its educational-permission carve-out stated as excluding AI-related uses. NEITHER IS A BOT-DETECTION PROBLEM, so the Bright Data connector was the wrong tool regardless of availability (and it is not exposed in my toolset anyway) -- the permission is explicitly refused, and routing around a written prohibition with better scraping tooling makes it deliberate rather than incidental. Reported this and raised the fork rather than building around it; Michael chose to swap in a legitimate substitute and state the gaps. ONE MORE FACT THAT SHARPENED IT: CanLII sued an AI legal-research platform (Caseway AI, Nov 2024) over systematic scraping -- live enforcement against exactly this product category, and confirmation that CanLII's KEYED API is the sanctioned route while scraping is not. SOURCES ACTUALLY USED, each under terms that permit it: US via the existing CourtListener citator; UK England & Wales via Find Case Law (The National Archives) -- real public API, no auth, verified live returning real August 2026 judgments, content under the Open Justice Licence which expressly permits commercial use and product incorporation, BUT that licence does NOT cover "computational analysis" (bulk programmatic searching to extract or enrich), so the client is on-demand lookup only and never sweeps the corpus; Canada via CanLII's official keyed API, verified live as real (403 ACCESS_DENIED without a key), degrading honestly to NOT_CONFIGURED. Australia and Ireland/Scotland are NOT COVERED and the app says so with the reason, rather than implying worldwide reach. PHASE A: Wex chosen deliberately over Black's (commercial, no free API) and Bouvier's (1856, wrong for modern/statutory terms). robots.txt read directly -- /wex is NOT disallowed, only Drupal paths and /search/, and Crawl-delay: 10 applies and is really enforced via a Supabase-backed ledger. Parser verified against the real live negligence page (13 paragraphs, smart quotes decoded, cross-refs extracted). No success shape exists without a real source_url. A REAL BUG CAUGHT DURING LIVE VERIFICATION, not by reading: with the migration unrun, define and intl_search returned an opaque 502 "Upstream connection error," which reads as a flaky network when the real cause is an un-run migration. Isolated it by evidence -- coverage (no table) returned 200, the two ledger-backed paths 502, and canlii_browse 503 because its key gate precedes the ledger check -- and fixed both to return 503 NOT_PROVISIONED naming the SQL file, matching every other SAIRN resource. Still FAILS CLOSED: a missing ledger means the published crawl-delay cannot be honoured, so the lookup is refused rather than running unthrottled. PHASE C, MOCK TRIAL: the refusal of outcome prediction is a REAL MECHANICAL CHECK on the model's own output (mtAssertNoProbability), not a prompt instruction -- a response containing a percentage, odds, or likelihood-of-winning claim is WITHHELD ENTIRELY and the user told why. Same fabricated-confidence problem already stripped from SAIRNcode's Fraud panel, worse here because it would advise legal strategy with no labelled outcome corpus behind it. Every reporter citation the adversary produces is extracted and checked against the real citator; anything the citator cannot find is labelled "Not verified -- do not rely on this" rather than shown as authority. PHASE D, JURY: two deliberately separate pieces -- difficulty sliders labelled in the UI as TRAINING settings for a practice juror (not a claim about any real panel), and an attitude-and-experience instrument built that way because the jury-consulting field's own research, verified before designing, finds demographics are poor predictors while attitudes/beliefs/experience predict considerably better, demographics being "at best an initial guide" and "inadequate for predicting juror behavior." THE HARD GUARDRAIL IS ENFORCED IN CODE: juryCheckStrikeReason() refuses any strike reason resting on race or gender and names the controlling authority. Word-boundary matched on purpose -- "management" must not trip "man", "Whitewater" must not trip "white" -- because a guardrail that nags on legitimate reasons trains users to ignore it, which is worse than not having one. Strike records additionally require an anchor in what the juror actually said, since an untethered reason is exactly what fails at Batson step three. ALL FOUR AUTHORITIES VERIFIED AGAINST PRIMARY SOURCES BEFORE ENCODING, and one materially widened scope: Batson v. Kentucky 476 U.S. 79 (1986); EDMONSON v. LEESVILLE CONCRETE CO. 500 U.S. 614 (1991), which extends Batson to CIVIL cases and private litigants -- so this applies to SAIRNlaw's civil users, not only criminal, which the brief did not mention; Georgia v. McCollum 505 U.S. 42 (1992); J.E.B. v. Alabama ex rel. T.B. 511 U.S. 127 (1994). Each carries a real Cornell LII source URL in the app. VERIFIED: node --check clean throughout; div balance 900/900; 19 nav / 19 panels 1:1; zero duplicate function names; dead-button audit 115 handlers / 0 missing; no dormant code (refRenderCoverage, mtRenderMatterOptions, juryRenderAll all wired into nav()). Isolated-logic tested 57/57 against the Phase C/D core extracted VERBATIM from the DEPLOYED sairnlaw.html rather than a scratch copy -- six outcome-prediction phrasings blocked and five legitimate critiques allowed, eight discriminatory strike reasons blocked and six legitimate ones allowed, five word-boundary false-positive cases, the anchor requirement, and that no voir-dire prompt anywhere references race or gender. TWO FALSE ALARMS INVESTIGATED RATHER THAN REPORTED AS BUGS: a "duplicate mtRun" (my grep matched the longer pre-existing mtRunConflictCheck) and a duplicate id tr-explain-result (pre-existing, created dynamically, present before this change) -- both checked against the pre-change file before concluding. ONE REAL TEST BUG found and fixed: an unanchored /age/ assertion matched "damAGEs_attitude"; corrected to word-boundary matching and then proven to still catch a deliberately planted demographic dimension rather than passing vacuously. TWO BLOCKERS, NEITHER MINE: sql/sairnlaw_wex_intl_schema.sql needs running, and CANLII_API_KEY needs a free key from CanLII. Until then Wex and Find Case Law return an honest NOT_PROVISIONED and Canada is honestly reported unavailable rather than silently empty. 2026-08-21
- api/_lib/deadline-engine.js + api/legal-deadlines.js + api/_resources/sairnlaw.js + api/sd-data.js + sql/sairnlaw_deadline_rules_schema.sql + sql/sairnlaw_deadline_seed_us_federal.json + sairnlaw.html (Hank) -- SAIRNLAW DEADLINE RULES ENGINE PHASE 1 DONE, pushed (`f17bf99`, corrected by `92c4783`), Guardian v2 full scan clean, live-verified. THE 6(d) VERIFICATION RESOLVED AND CONFIRMED THE ASSUMPTION -- reporting because the instruction was to report either way: the 2005 Advisory Committee Note states "Three days are added after the prescribed period otherwise expires under Rule 6(a). Intermediate Saturdays, Sundays, and legal holidays are included in counting these added three days. If the third day is a Saturday, Sunday, or legal holiday, the last day to act is the next day that is not a Saturday, Sunday, or legal holiday." So the order is 6(a) period -> roll -> +3 calendar days -> roll AGAIN, a double rollover exactly as designed. EXTENSION_UNVERIFIED lifted, path enabled. I MADE A REAL DESIGN ERROR AND PRODUCTION CAUGHT IT, disclosed rather than quietly patched: my spec said "api/sd-data.js deliberately untouched -- the per-app registry split means new resources go in api/_resources/sairnlaw.js alone," and Michael approved that. It is WRONG. Registering the name passes the allowlist but a resource with no HANDLER BRANCH falls through to "Unsupported action/resource combination" -- so after the first push law_deadlines still returned 400, just with a different message. The registry split removed the shared-map MERGE-COLLISION surface; it never removed the handler requirement, and my own _resources/sairnlaw.js header already said "add the name here, and add its handler branch in api/sd-data.js as before" -- the spec line contradicted the file it described and I did not catch it until production said so. Corrected in `92c4783`: a law_deadlines read/write branch added to sd-data.js (pure addition, 39 lines, ZERO removed, CRLF preserved via the established autocrlf-safe procedure), and law_deadline_rules + law_holidays REMOVED from the registry because they are read/written by api/legal-deadlines.js talking to Supabase directly and never pass through sd-data.js -- registering them there made them look supported while actually producing the same "Unsupported" failure, which is worse than an honest unrecognised-resource error. Registry is 159, not 161. THE BLOCKER IS NOW GENUINELY FIXED, verified live: law_deadlines returns 200 {"ok":true,"data":[],"provisioned":false} -- an honest "table not created yet" -- where it returned 400 before. Every deadline in SAIRNlaw had been living on exactly one browser, never hydrated back, lost with the profile. ENGINE: api/_lib/deadline-engine.js is PURE (no I/O) so date arithmetic is testable against worked examples from the rule text without a database; all dates handled in UTC because local-time Date arithmetic is how off-by-one date bugs happen and this platform has already had one. FRAP 26(a) and Bankruptcy 9006(a) MAP TO the FRCP 6(a) implementation rather than getting copies that could drift. WHAT VERIFICATION CHANGED versus an implementation from memory: FRCP counts CALENDAR days (6(a)(1)(B) counts every intermediate weekend/holiday, only the LAST day rolls -- implementing it as business days errs LATER than reality, the dangerous direction, and the endpoint now REJECTS any rule pairing an FRCP-family standard with business_days); the pre-2009 sub-11-day weekend exclusion is OBSOLETE and not applied; 6(a)(6) is DIRECTION-DEPENDENT so state holidays count only for forward periods and each holiday carries a `kind` the engine consults against direction; 6(a)(5) backward periods roll BACKWARD. FAILS CLOSED with every refusal naming the missing thing -- NOT_PROVISIONED, NO_MATCHING_RULE (lists the triggers that ARE covered), NO_RULE_IN_FORCE (lists the windows), AMBIGUOUS_RULE (refuses rather than picking one of two overlapping rules), UNKNOWN_STANDARD, UNKNOWN_UNIT -- and no failure path returns a due_date, asserted by test. Nothing is computed from today's date and no trigger is ever inferred. The cross-year case works as specced: a 21-day period triggered 20 Dec crosses into the next year and the refusal names 2027, not the trigger year. RULES ARE VERSIONED DATA with required authority.citation + resolvable URL, verified_by stamped SERVER-SIDE from the real session, and required effective_from/effective_to so a matter triggered in 2023 computes against the 2023 rule. SEED is US Federal civil only, no scope expansion, every day count read from the primary source 2026-08-21 and QUOTED in the row. TWO DELIBERATE OMISSIONS WITH REASONS: Rule 12(a)(3) is NOT encoded because it runs from service on the officer or on the US attorney "whichever is later" -- a two-trigger rule the engine does not model, so encoding it would compute from whichever trigger was supplied and be too early roughly half the time; and Rule 6(d) is NOT attached to the waiver deadlines because 6(d) applies when a party must act "after being SERVED" while the waiver clock runs from when the request was SENT -- attaching it would extend a deadline the rule does not extend, verified by test (waiver + mail returns the same date). VERIFIED: Guardian v2 full scan clean -- 0 direct API calls, 0 service_role, 0 box chars in JS strings (28 in comment banners are pre-existing house style), 0 real duplicate ids, 0 undefined handlers, 0 duplicate function names, 20 nav / 20 panels 1:1, all localStorage keys law_ prefixed, 0 hardcoded keys, 0 console.log, div balance 945/945, dead-button audit 0 flagged across all 7 sections, session token scoped to 'sairnlaw' (Check 28). Engine isolated-logic tested 33/33 against worked examples from the rule text including a PLANTED-FAILURE check proving the holiday test can still fail; seed validated 5/5 against the real endpoint validator. A THIRD REGEX FALSE POSITIVE INVESTIGATED RATHER THAN REPORTED AS A BUG: the "duplicate id" tr-explain-result is two mutually-exclusive outerHTML branches inside JS string literals, and outerHTML REPLACES the node so only one ever exists -- confirmed by re-running the check against markup only (314 ids, zero real duplicates). BLOCKERS, NOT MINE: sql/sairnlaw_deadline_rules_schema.sql not yet run and the seed not yet loaded -- both degrade to an honest 503 NOT_PROVISIONED naming the SQL file. 2026-08-21

- sairnlaw.html + docs/sairnlaw-ohio-efiling-scope.md (Hank) — DONE. Court e-filing, Ohio-first: the last open roadmap item. RESEARCH FINDING FIRST: there is no way to transmit a filing into an Ohio court from this app. Ohio has no statewide trial-court e-filing system (Civ.R. 5(E) + Sup.R. 27 = county-by-county local rules), counties run different vendors (Tyler/Odyssey, Henschen, county-built), and programmatic filing requires certification as an EFSP -- a contract, not an API key. Even the Supreme Court of Ohio publishes a web portal only. So NOT a filing transmitter. Built an E-Filing Readiness Check instead: inspects the actual PDF bytes and checks them against the real published rules of 2 courts (Supreme Court of Ohio; Franklin County C.P. General Division), every rule carrying its citation. Three-state results -- PASS / FAIL / CANNOT VERIFY -- because margins, line spacing and "exclusive of the table of contents" page counts cannot be read from PDF bytes and reporting them as passes would be the exact false-confidence failure this module exists to avoid. 49/49 isolation tests. Panel says on screen that it files nothing. 2026-08-23
- api/_lib/deadline-engine.js + api/legal-deadlines.js + sql/sairnlaw_deadline_{seed,calendars}_illinois.json (Hank) — DONE (code); LOADING next. Deadline engine jurisdiction #6: ILLINOIS, first of the market-size Batch 1 (IL -> FL -> CA). Every rule text read verbatim end to end before encoding. Illinois differs from all five existing jurisdictions in three ways that would silently produce wrong dates if copied: (1) 5 ILCS 70/1.11 has NO short-period exclusion, so it follows MI/PA not OH/IN; (2) 205 ILCS 630/17(a) states NO weekend observance shift at all, unlike federal/IN/MI/PA (both-way) and OH (Sunday-only); (3) Good Friday is a statutory holiday requiring real Easter computation, and general election day is statutory in even years only. DISCLOSED NOT RECONCILED: the statutory holiday list and the list Illinois COURTS observe (S.Ct. order M.R. 5272) disagree in BOTH directions -- M.R. 5272 omits Pulaski Day and Good Friday, and adds the Day After Thanksgiving. Calendars encode the STATUTORY list because 1.11 keys on statute, not on whether the clerk is open. 39/39 isolation tests. 2026-08-23

Deadline jurisdiction #7: FLORIDA. SCOPE CORRECTION: I proposed Florida as "data-only, no engine change". WRONG. Fla. R. Gen. Prac. & Jud. Admin. 2.514(a)(1)(A) says "begin counting from the next day that is not a Saturday, Sunday, or legal holiday" -- Florida SHIFTS THE START to the next business day and counts it as day one, where FRCP 6(a)(1)(A) merely excludes the trigger day. Every Florida deadline triggered on a Friday or the day before a holiday differs. New data-driven shifted_start property on the standard (not a new impl string), so a future state with this shape needs no code. Also new: fl_rgpja_2514b service extension = FIVE days for mail only, not the FRCP three. Holiday list is the RULE (2.514(a)(6)(A), nine enumerated observances), NOT all of Fla. Stat. 110.117 -- no Columbus Day, Presidents Day, Juneteenth or Good Friday. Chief-justice hurricane-extension limb deliberately NOT modelled, flagged in 3 places. 44/44 Florida + 39/39 Illinois still pass. Also created docs/SAIRN-OPEN-WORK-INDEX.md, one live index of every open item platform-wide. 2026-08-23

THE 2025 RESTYLING IS THE STORY -- adopted 2024-01-31, effective 2025-01-01, first substantial revision since 1998, and the FORMER rules were read verbatim specifically to find out what changed rather than assuming the new text had always said it. Three changes, each of which the old rule would have encoded wrong: the short-period exclusion is gone (and it was "fewer than 11 days", not the 7 Ohio/Indiana/Georgia use, so it could not have been carried across from them); the service-extension SEQUENCING FLIPPED from "added to the prescribed period" to "added after the period would otherwise expire" -- FIRST JURISDICTION HERE WHOSE SEQUENCING CHANGED OVER TIME, so the standing habit has to be read per DATE not just per state; and the same state sequences its two extensions OPPOSITE WAYS AT ONCE, civil 6(e) after-expiry vs appellate 39(c) period-lengthening, so it also has to be read per BODY OF RULES. RULE 6(e) POINTS AT THE WRONG SUBPARAGRAPH AND THE ENGINE NOW REFUSES RATHER THAN PICKING A SIDE: WV inserted a state-specific 5(b)(2)(E) (WV E-Filing/fax) that pushed the federal lettering down one, so 6(e)'s pointer "(F)" now names "other ELECTRONIC means consented in writing" while its parenthetical "(other means consented to)" describes (G). Read by the pointer WV extends for consented e-service, which the federal rule stopped doing in 2016; read by the parenthetical it does not. New engine state refused_contested_standard reports both contested methods visibly -- the usual "prefer the earlier date" tie-break would mean adding nothing, and a silent no-extension is indistinguishable from "none requested", the exact defect this engine already fixed once. Verified against a SECOND independent rendering on the court's own site before building code on it. ONE CALENDAR CANNOT SERVE BOTH HOLIDAY LISTS: R. App. P. 39(a) names Juneteenth, R. Civ. P. 6(a)(6) does not and CANNOT reach it because 6(a)(6)(C) omits CONGRESS from the bodies whose declarations count. Calendars key on jurisdiction+year only, so the wv calendar encodes the CIVIL list and a WV appellate deadline landing on 19 June computes ONE DAY EARLY -- chosen because the opposite rolls civil deadlines LATE, per the Pennsylvania rule already in the engine header. "LINCOLN'S DAY" IS THE DAY AFTER THANKSGIVING, not 12 February: 6(a)(6)(A) says "Lincoln's Birthday" but counts "the day set aside BY STATUTE", and 2-2-1(a)(11) puts it after Thanksgiving. KENTUCKY, TWO INDEPENDENT BLOCKERS, NEITHER A FAILURE TO TRY: (1) kycourts.gov DOES NOT PUBLISH the Rules of Civil Procedure -- its rules page carries one link and it points to Thomson Reuters Westlaw. Christmas is THREE days and Thanksgiving TWO, allocated administratively -- no rule produces those dates, so the calendar must never be generated. Rule 36 is the dangerous one -- SELF-EXECUTING, so a row built on 45 would concede facts 15 days before the defendant had to answer; a live compute proves 33 and 36 diverge on identical facts (2026-07-16 vs 2026-07-31). No later-of row carries a service extension, and that is a reason: limb one is Rule 5 service which 6(e) reaches, limb two is Rule 4 service which it does not, and the engine extends the winning RESULT without knowing which limb won. Rule 6(e) is MAIL ONLY and LENGTHENS THE PERIOD -- live-verified at 2026-06-03 where the federal order gives 2026-06-04. NOT SEEDED: all backward periods (Rule 6(a) defines the rollover only forward, and WV/Florida roll backward while KRS 446.030(1)(b) rolls FORWARD, so there is no default), NC appellate (needs its own source check -- the Rules of Appellate Procedure are court rules, not statutes), and Rule 6(f)'s 5-day ACP extension (its amount is conditioned on the BASE PERIOD LENGTH, which amount(method) cannot see). 54/54 NC + 51/51 WV + all 17 other api/_lib suites pass; every rule and calendar run through the REAL endpoint validators. No wrong dates result (an absent jurisdiction refuses), which is why it went unnoticed. Michael's call which tenant is canonical. A junk zz/__probe__ row is now live and this endpoint implements no delete. Lesson recorded in the loader's own docstring: never probe a deployment with a write endpoint; use rules_status. Also learned and recorded there: push and wait for the deploy before loading a jurisdiction that needs a new computation standard -- NC's first load rejected all 13 rules against a deployment that predated nc_rcp_6a while both calendars loaded, the exact half-load the loader orders calendars-first to minimise. 2026-08-25

GATE RUN ON BOTH BEFORE ANY CODE, which is the whole point of adding it after Kentucky. WASHINGTON PASSED OUTRIGHT: its Superior Court Civil Rules are published free and in full, one PDF per rule, at courts.wa.gov. WASHINGTON IS THE ONE STATE THAT SETTLES THE HOLIDAY QUESTION BY TEXT -- CR 6(a) says, mid-rule, "Legal holidays are prescribed in RCW 1.16.050", an EXPRESS cross-reference no other seeded jurisdiction provides (Texas says "legal holiday" and never cites 662.021; Kentucky says it twice and defines it nowhere; WV defines it two ways in one state; NC asks whether the COURTHOUSE IS CLOSED). AND THE STATUTE EXCLUDES ITS OWN DECOYS: 1.16.050(1) is the legal holidays, 1.16.050(7) declares Columbus Day, Pearl Harbor Day, Marcus Whitman Day and a dozen more "recognized ... but may not be considered legal holidays FOR ANY PURPOSE". Only (1) encoded; two tests assert (7) days are absent, and a live compute proves Columbus Day 2026 does not roll. SUNDAY IS A LEGAL HOLIDAY under 1.16.050(1)(a) and is deliberately unlisted -- the engine excludes weekends before consulting a calendar, so 52 entries/year would change no date; it also explains why 1.16.050(5)(a) shifts a Sunday holiday "OTHER THAN SUNDAY". Both-way shift; the 2028 New Year lands Fri 2027-12-31 and is filed under 2027 only, so 2028 has ten entries and the others eleven or twelve. ALL THREE DISCOVERY RULES USE 30/40, and that is a fact about Washington rather than a pattern: NORTH CAROLINA'S THREE DIVERGE AT 45/45/60. CR 36 is where an error hurts most (self-executing), so importing NC's 60 would concede facts twenty days late. CR 6(e) IS MAIL ONLY AND LENGTHENS THE PERIOD, read up front; live-verified 2026-06-03 where the federal order gives 06-04. FIVE STATES NOW ANSWER E-SERVICE FIVE DIFFERENT WAYS (KY expressly adds 3, WV contested and refused in code, CA adds 2 COURT days, NC and WA don't cover it). effective_from is each RULE'S OWN last amendment date off its published footer -- better provenance than NC's blanket 1970 -- and CR 12 was amended as recently as 2025-09-01. NOT SEEDED: all backward periods (CR 6(d)'s 5-day notice and 1-day affidavit are backward AND both under the 7-day threshold; CR 6(a) defines the rollover only forward and the three jurisdictions that do define it disagree), and appellate pending its own gate. 48/48 new suite + 54/54 NC + 51/51 WV + every other api/_lib suite; 14 rules and 6 calendars through the real validators; loaded and 9/9 live computes. The index itself stops at 2024, so even reconstruction carries an unknown 2025-26 gap. Failed anyway because a reconstruction cannot prove its own completeness and a missed amendment yields wrong dates with NO refusal. BANKED VERBATIM: AZ Rule 6's short-period exclusion is ELEVEN days -- higher than any state seeded, all of which are 7, so encoding 7 by analogy would be wrong on every 7-10 day AZ period; Rule 6(a)(4) DEFINES THE BACKWARD DIRECTION expressly, so AZ backward rows would be seedable where NC's and WA's were not; Rule 6(c) adds FIVE days on the FEDERAL after-expiry order, matching only Florida. ONE DECISION -- reading Westlaw's terms once -- UNBLOCKS ARIZONA AND KENTUCKY TOGETHER. Also added AZ to the bundled holiday question, which now spans five jurisdictions and has a sharper framing: WA and NC have each answered it themselves and answered it DIFFERENTLY (express statute vs courthouse-closure test), so the real question is which source a rule's own words point at and what to do when they point at nothing. 2026-08-25
- api/_lib/deadline-engine.js + api/legal-deadlines.js + api/_lib/deadline-virginia.test.js + sql/sairnlaw_deadline_{seed,calendars}_virginia.json + tools/gen_va_{seed,calendar}.py + tools/va_rule_currency.py + docs/SAIRN-OPEN-WORK-INDEX.md (Hank) — VIRGINIA DONE, committed (`73735ab`), LOADED and LIVE-VERIFIED. 167 -> 190 rules, 15 -> 16 jurisdictions. TWO FIRSTS, AND THEY ARE THE STORY. (1) THE COMPUTATION AUTHORITY IS A STATUTE THAT REACHES STATUTES: Va. Code 1-210 computes time fixed by "an act of the General Assembly OR rule of court" -- the EXACT INVERSE of New Jersey's R. 1:3-1, which reaches time fixed "by rule or court order" and NOT by statute. The two were read independently and neither assumed from the other; a VA deadline fixed by statute is INSIDE this standard where the equivalent NJ one is outside. NO SHORT-PERIOD EXCLUSION ANYWHERE, checked against the full published Rules rather than inherited from the six seeded states that have one. (2) FIRST TIME-OF-DAY SERVICE EXTENSION: Va. Sup. Ct. R. 1:7 adds 0 days at/before 5:00 p.m. and 1 day after, FOR THE SAME METHOD -- manual delivery, fax, email and same-day commercial all swing on the clock alone. amount(method) could not express it, so amount() now takes a CONTEXT: amount(method, ctx) with ctx.service_time, ctx.base_period_count, ctx.base_period_unit. A MISSING service_time IS REFUSED, NOT GUESSED (new refused_missing_context state) -- defaulting to 0 runs EARLY and to 1 runs LATE, the rule allows both, and this is the only refusal in the file the CALLER can fix, so the message says exactly how. COMMERCIAL DELIVERY IS TWO METHODS because the rule splits by SERVICE BOUGHT not by clock: same-day follows the 5pm cutoff, next-day adds 1 whenever handed over before midnight; a bare 'commercial_delivery' deliberately does not qualify. ONE ADDITION SERVED BOTH BLOCKED RULES -- N.C. R. Civ. P. 6(f) was documented as blocked on this same missing shape and the shape was built general on purpose. 6(f) IS STILL NOT IMPLEMENTED AND ITS BLOCKER MOVED RATHER THAN VANISHED: it also needs to know whether the served party is an Address Confidentiality Program participant, a fact about the PARTY that this engine accepts no input for, and ACP status is confidential by the program's own design so trusting a caller to assert it is a real decision, not a default. Given its OWN index row so the next session does not re-derive it, and the stale clause in the NC gate row that still called it an amount() problem was corrected in place. THE 1-210(F) GAP IS DISCLOSED, NOT REFUSED, AND THE DIRECTION IS WHY: 1-210(F) makes any day the Governor authorizes closing state government a legal holiday, and 17.1-207 lets each clerk close for locality holidays, Christmas Eve and judge-authorized health/safety threats -- none knowable in advance, all per-locality. Omitting them can only report a date EARLIER than the true deadline, never later, so a new top-level JURISDICTION_COVERAGE block rides on every successful VA result instead of blocking it. That is the OPPOSITE call from Kentucky, whose KRS 2.110 gap runs LATE and therefore refuses -- the new table's header states the refuse-vs-disclose test explicitly so the next jurisdiction is decided, not guessed. GUARDIAN CHECK 29 CAUGHT A REAL BUG BEFORE IT SHIPPED, the exact California class: gen_va_calendar.py emitted kind:"derived", which is NOT in validateHolidayPayload's enum (federal/declared/state) -- all six VA calendar years would have been rejected by add_holidays while every engine unit test stayed green, because the engine and its validator are separate files and unit tests never touch the validator. Every sibling jurisdiction uses "declared". Fixed, then all 12 rule payloads and 6 holiday-year payloads were run through the REAL endpoint validators extracted from api/legal-deadlines.js, 0 rejected. EFFECTIVE_FROM IS REAL ON EVERY ROW, which NJ and NC could not manage (both carry a blanket 1969-09-08 and disclose it): the published Rules print a per-rule "Last amended by Order dated ...; effective ..." line and tools/va_rule_currency.py scopes STRICTLY from one rule heading to the next so no rule inherits its neighbour's date. CALENDAR IS SIX YEARS not one or two, because VA holidays are fixed by STATUTE in derivable terms (2.2-3300) rather than by an annual court order (NJ) or a published administrative schedule (NC) -- every date DERIVED, each carrying its own derivation string, generator asserting no duplicate and no weekend date per year. ELECTION DAY IS ANNUAL NOT EVEN-YEAR (VA elects its House of Delegates in odd years, same trap as NJ) and the DAY AFTER THANKSGIVING IS IN THE STATUTE ITSELF, unlike NC's administrative blocks. 47/47 in a new isolated suite against the real engine and real JSON on disk, every date worked by hand from the rule text first, including the 17:00 boundary minute and a North Carolina cross-check proving the new ctx argument does not regress a pre-existing standard; all other api/_lib suites still pass. LIVE-VERIFIED against the deployed endpoint, not assumed from the push: rules_status shows va = 12 rules / 2026-2031, the coverage block appears on a successful compute with NC returning coverage:null as control, and 17:00 -> +0 / 17:01 -> +1 confirmed live. 2026-08-25

Adding next July's order makes 2028 emit with no other change. Generator reproduces the hand-built 2026 calendar BYTE-IDENTICALLY, so 2027 is provably additive. The three NJ tests that asserted the gap now assert the fix and were KEPT not deleted -- the property they guard is unchanged. 56 -> 74. MASSACHUSETTS: gate PASSED (docs/sairnlaw-massachusetts-deadline-seed-gate.md) and seeded same session. R. 6(a) ANSWERS THE HOLIDAY QUESTION IN ITS OWN TEXT -- "'legal holiday' includes those days specified in Mass. G.L. c. 4, s. 7" -- an EXPRESS cross-reference like Washington's CR 6(a) -> RCW 1.16.050 and the opposite of TX/AZ/KY, so MA does NOT join the bundled lawyer's question. Encoded statewide and DISCLOSED: including them rolls thirteen counties LATE, omitting them can only run EARLY in Suffolk, AND the same clause requires Suffolk offices to stay "open for business and appropriately staffed" on both days, so the omission may simply be CORRECT -- safe on both readings. Disclosure names BOSTON out loud so nobody reads it as an edge case. THE GENERATOR'S OWN ASSERTION CAUGHT A CARRIED-OVER ASSUMPTION AND THE ASSERTION WAS THE THING THAT WAS WRONG: the weekend-shift check copied from the VA/WV generators fired on 2026-07-04, because Cl. 18 shifts only "when any of said days occurs on Sunday" and is SILENT ON SATURDAY -- so 4 July 2026 really is a Saturday legal holiday in MA. Harmless downstream (R. 6(a) rolls off a Saturday in its own right) but a both-ways shift carried across would have INVENTED A FRIDAY HOLIDAY and rolled deadlines LATE. Check now asserts no SUNDAY can appear, which is what the statute actually guarantees.
