Different subject, **same 180KB single file**, which is precisely the write collision the per-clone split exists to avoid. The bug stands as reported: the agreement panel renders `String(rescinded_at).slice(0,10)` and `String(created_at).slice(0,10)`, reading the UTC calendar day off a correctly-stored instant -- **a DISPLAY-side instance of the class fixed on the storage side in `3d65fa9`**, which the scanner does not look for and which no app on the platform has been swept for. Available to pick up the moment Cody releases. 2026-09-02
- sairnlaw.html + api/_lib/sairnlaw-grounded-drafting.test.js (Hank, 2026-09-02) -- **THE DRAFTING AND REVIEW SURFACES ARE GROUNDED TOO, BY RETRIEVING FIRST RATHER THAN BY ORDERING A TOOL CALL (`271d318`). 43/43, six negative controls, live-verified.**
  **MICHAEL'S CALL ON THE MECHANISM, AND IT WAS THE RIGHT ONE.** I had flagged that forcing `define_legal_term` on these two would be wrong; both send ONE `ai_generate` call with **no `tools` field**, so the instruction would order them to reach something that is not there -- a rule that reads as a guard and enforces nothing. **Inverted instead: retrieve, inject the real text and its source URL into the system prompt, forbid anything outside it.**
  **THE PROHIBITION IS THE GUARANTEE, THE INJECTION IS ONLY THE COVERAGE, and that split is why a heuristic is acceptable here.** Term extraction from free text will miss things. When it misses there is **no grounded material** for that term, and `LAW_GROUNDED_DEFINITIONS_RULE` then requires the model to say so and carry on. **A missed extraction costs a refusal, never a fabrication.**
  **EXTRACTION IS DETERMINISTIC AND NARROW: neither a model judgment nor a hardcoded glossary**, either of which would reintroduce the thing being removed. It fires only where the text ITSELF marks a term as a term -- `define X`, `what does X mean`, `the meaning of X`, `the doctrine of X`, or a quoted phrase, which is how a real document flags a term of art. Precision over recall deliberately: the 2026-08-30 SAIRNsenior sweep is the recorded case of a broad pattern producing 42 hits and one real finding.
  **THREE NORMALISATION BUGS WERE FOUND BY RUNNING THE EXTRACTOR ON PROSE IT HAD NOT BEEN ASSERTED AGAINST -- the whole reason that step exists, and the first version passed 38/38 without them.** `"What is a novation?"` extracted **`a novation`**, which slugs to `a_novation` and always misses. `"What is our next step?"` extracted **`our next step`** and would have spent the single available lookup on it. And `"the doctrine of laches and the meaning of estoppel"` **LOST laches entirely** -- no punctuation until the full stop, so the capture ran to five words and was rejected for length. Fixed by stripping a leading article, **REJECTING** a possessive-led capture rather than stripping it (removing the determiner leaves prose, not a term), and ending a capture at `" and "`/`" or "`. **A fourth came out of the fix itself:** `"the doctrine of unclean hands"` needs the article stripped **BEFORE** the framing noun, or the framing strip anchors on `the`, never fires, and both lookups go to the same term -- once as a phrase that misses. That ordering is now asserted.
  **THE RATE-LIMIT SHORT-CIRCUIT IS PROVEN BY CALL COUNT, NOT BY READING THE CODE.** Cornell LII publishes `Crawl-delay: 10` and `api/_lib/wex.js` honours it deployment-wide, so once one lookup is refused the next in the same second will be too. The test stubs `referenceFetch`, **counts the calls, and asserts exactly one is issued**.
  **EVERY OUTCOME IS SHOWN TO THE USER, including "nothing was looked up" and why that is safe** -- a grounding step that runs invisibly is indistinguishable from one that silently did nothing, which is the failure this app keeps finding.
  **AND THE RULE PROTECTS LEGITIMATE DRAFTING, asserted so a later tightening cannot quietly break it:** writing a contract clause that defines a term for the parties is drafting, and saying a document uses a term inconsistently is review. Neither is asserting what a term means as a matter of law.
  **VERIFIED: 43/43, 68 test files 0 failing, sairnlaw.html 2/2 blocks, 314 globals 0 duplicates, divs 1029/1029, CRLF preserved, 0 stray CRs. Negative control, file restored byte-identical: unwiring the rule from drafting fails 1, from review fails 1, removing the rate-limit short-circuit fails 2, hiding the grounding note fails 1, dropping the drafting carve-out fails 1, reversing the normalisation order fails 2. LIVE on `sairn.vercel.app/sairnlaw`: rule declared, both surfaces retrieve first, 2 splice sites, extractor and short-circuit present.** 2026-09-02
- sairnsenior.html + sql/sairnsenior_claims_schema.sql + api/_lib/sairnsenior-appeals.test.js (Hank, 2026-09-02) -- **COMPETITIVE-GAP ITEM B2 BUILT AND LIVE (`850b0b3`): DENIALS GET AN APPEAL, AND `denied` STOPS BEING A DEAD END. 46/46, eight negative controls, 10/10 live checks.**
  **WHY SAIRNSENIOR:** CC is on StoneDesk's audit, Cody on SAIRNroofing's subcontractor layer, Fourth on SAIRNgrounds -- **SAIRNsenior is the one app in the 2026-08-26 audit nobody is on.** B2's evidence line: *"No vendor in the market published a concrete denials-and-appeals workflow"*, with *"Medicaid billing opacity"* the loudest single Tier B complaint.
  **THE AUDIT'S LINE WAS HALF STALE AND I CHECKED THE FILE BEFORE BUILDING.** Denial reasons were already captured and denial PATTERNS already analysed with a real 2-claim minimum. The missing half was the other one: **a denied claim had no action button at all**, so the money was written off by the interface rather than by anyone deciding to.
  **TWO DESIGN CALLS, BOTH ABOUT NOT OVERSTATING MONEY. (1) AN OVERTURNED APPEAL RETURNS THE CLAIM TO `submitted`, NOT `paid`** -- the payer agreeing to reprocess is not the payer having paid, and the existing Mark Paid step is what records money arriving. Booking the overturn as paid would put a figure on the Billing screen that no bank statement backs. **(2) "Recovered by Appeal" therefore counts only appealed + overturned + SINCE PAID**, and the tile says so on its face so the number cannot be misread as won-appeals.
  **THE COUNTING TRAP IS THE REASON THE SUITE EXISTS.** The appeal history counts a claim as denied-**EVER** by whether it carries a denial reason, not by its current status. Filtering on `status==='denied'` -- which the older pattern breakdown does, **correctly, for its own question** -- **drops every appeal that SUCCEEDED**, because a won appeal moves the claim to submitted and then paid. That version reports a **0% overturn rate no matter how many were won**, which is the exact wrong answer to *"is appealing worth it"*. Driven directly: two won, one lost, one pending, and the rate must read **66.7%**.
  **NO DEADLINE IS EVER DERIVED.** Appeal-filing windows come from payer contracts and state Medicaid rules and this app does not know them. The deadline is **OPTIONAL** on the denial, a denied claim without one is reported as `without_recorded_deadline` rather than given a default, and the countdown only ever runs on a date the agency typed in. **A required field would just make somebody type a plausible date, and a guessed deadline on money still recoverable is worse than none.** The AI tool's description forbids stating or estimating any payer's window.
  **THE RATE IS OVER DECIDED APPEALS ONLY**, so filing a new one cannot lower it, and a reason with only a pending appeal reports **no rate rather than a zero**. An empty appeal history returns a note saying explicitly that **it is not evidence appeals do not work** -- and the tool description forbids reading it that way.
  **NO SCHEMA CHANGE AND NO SERVER CHANGE.** `sen_claims` stores `data` as free-form jsonb and `api/sd-data.js` writes it whole, so the SQL touched is its **column comment only**. The client caps the appeal basis at 1000 chars and the note fields at 300 because that table carries a **64KB per-claim CHECK** -- a pasted denial letter is exactly how a row crosses it, and the write would 400 while the claim still looked saved locally. Same class as SAIRNdental's appointments payload guard.
  **VERIFIED: 46/46, 69 test files 0 failing, sairnsenior.html 1/1 block, 148 globals 0 duplicates, divs 431/431, 0 DOM targets referenced but absent, CRLF preserved, 0 stray CRs. Negative control, eight breaks, file restored byte-identical:** booking an overturn as paid fails 2, counting recovery on the overturn fails 3, **filtering the history on current status fails 5 -- as a hard stop, the breakdown going undefined, rather than a clean assertion, and that is stated rather than counted as an assertion it is not**, counting pending as losses fails 1, defaulting a missing deadline fails 1, dropping the basis requirement fails 2, removing the payload cap fails 1, restoring `denied` as a dead end fails 1. **LIVE on `sairn.vercel.app/sairnsenior`: 10/10 checks including that the overturn does not book as paid and that the history is not filtered on current status.**
  **ONE PRE-EXISTING SEAM REPORTED, NOT MINE AND NOT TOUCHED:** the push gate's check 4 says COULD NOT TELL on both of tonight's pushes. Run in full, it is **31 clean, 0 not-forwarded, 1 could-not-tell** -- `api/sd-data.js -> api/_lib/subcontractor-compliance.js canAssign()`, from Cody's `62aaf61`, which destructures its input so the tool cannot read what it depends on. **Neither of my commits touched an `api/*.js` endpoint.** It needs a hand-written input test like `deadline-endpoint-inputs.test.js`; flagged to whoever owns that layer. 2026-09-02
- api/_lib/deadline-engine.js + api/legal-deadlines.js + 9 seed files + api/_lib/deadline-limb-scope.test.js (Hank, 2026-09-02) -- **PER-LIMB SERVICE-EXTENSION SCOPING BUILT AND LIVE (`5f3fc4f`). 16 rows, 9 jurisdictions, LATE-direction defect closed. 28/28 + 83/83, four negative controls, and 16/16 live-verified in BOTH directions.**
  **THE DEFECT, RESTATED FROM THE MEASUREMENT RATHER THAN FROM THE HAWAII QUESTION THAT FOUND IT:** a service extension was declared per ROW and applied AFTER resolution, to whichever limb governed. Every multi-trigger row carrying one has a second limb no mail rule reaches -- **eleven run from service of the SUMMONS** (Rule 4 service, where all nine of these extension rules are gated on the Rule 5 analogue) and **Maryland's four run from `date_initial_pleading_is_required`, which is not a service event at all**. Fourteen of fifteen added the days there. **Ten of the fourteen are Rule 36 admissions rows**, where the matter is admitted unless answered and Rule 36(b) makes the admission conclusively established.
  **MICHAEL'S DECISION WAS BUILD IT PROPERLY RATHER THAN STRIP THE EXTENSIONS, and the measurement says that was right: 16 of 16 still add their days when the paper-service limb governs.** A blanket strip would have been safe and would have cost every one of those a real, earned extension.
  **NINE JURISDICTIONS, ONE ANSWER, THREE DIFFERENT ROUTES -- WHICH IS EXACTLY WHY EACH HAD TO BE READ RATHER THAN GENERALISED FROM HAWAII.** *Express subsection citation:* AL 6(d) "Rule 5(b)(2)(C) ... or (E)"; MT 6(d) "Rule 5(b)(2)(C),(D),(E),(F)"; NE § 6-1106(c) "under § 6-1105(b)(3)(C)", and § 6-1105 is titled **"Serving and filing pleadings and other documents"**; NM 1-006(C) "under **Rule 1-005(C)(1)(e) NMRA**", 1-005 being service of papers and **1-004** being process. *"A notice or other paper"* -- Rule 5 vocabulary with no subsection named: **HI 6(e), MD 1-203(c), WI § 801.15(5)**. *The same PLUS an express Rule 4 carve-out in the rule's own text:* **AR 6(d)** ("shall not extend the time in which the defendant must file an answer ... in accordance with Rule 4") and **MS 6(e)**.
  **MISSISSIPPI WAS NEVER A READING AND THAT IS THE UNCOMFORTABLE PART.** Miss. R. Civ. P. 6(e)'s final sentence -- *"This subdivision does not apply to responses to service of summons under Rule 4."* -- **was already quoted verbatim in its own standard's comment inside `deadline-engine.js`** while the row extended on exactly that limb. Alabama and Arkansas were contradicted by principles their own comments state and correctly apply to their ANSWER rows. **The text was in the file; nothing compared it to the row.**
  **PRIMARY SOURCES READ THIS SESSION, not taken from the captured comments:** Haw. R. Civ. P. 6 in full from the Judiciary's published rules; Wis. Stat. § 801.15 from docs.legis.wisconsin.gov; **NM Rule 1-006(C) from the Supreme Court's own approved-amendment PDF**, extracted from the PDF streams because the fetcher could not read it -- which also re-confirmed that "electronic transmission," is bracketed for deletion, matching the existing comment. Nebraska's § 6-1105 title, Maryland's 1-203(c), Arkansas's 6(d) proviso, Alabama's 6(d) and Mississippi's 6(e) confirmed against official or court-published text.
  **MECHANISM: `service_extension.applies_to_limbs`, DECLARED PER ROW AND NEVER DERIVED FROM THE LIMB'S NAME.** A default computed from another field is the invisible-rule shape the `trigger_document` work already rejected -- a limb called `service_of_summons_...` looks inferable right up until a state writes one that IS reached. **An undeclared scope REFUSES rather than defaulting either way:** defaulting to "reaches everything" is the bug, and defaulting to "reaches nothing" would silently drop days a row earns, on the same code path that used to add them. **A limb name that is not one of the rule's own also refuses**, because a misspelt entry would scope to nothing while looking deliberate -- the same silent-no-op class as a misspelt `on_unknown_exclusivity`.
  **THE WRITE PATH VALIDATES IT TOO, for the reason `api/legal-deadlines.js` already gives about `on_unknown_exclusivity`: nothing there rejects unknown fields**, so a misspelt KEY would be stored happily, the engine would see an undeclared scope and refuse, and the row would look correct in the store while quietly covering less. It also rejects a declaration naming EVERY limb -- the old behaviour written out longhand.
  **MONTANA GAINS AN EXTENSION IT HAD DELIBERATELY NOT CARRIED, HOURS AFTER SHIPPING WITHOUT IT.** Its Rule 36 row's note said why: the engine could only scope per row, so adding days when the 45-day floor governed reported LATE and omitting them reported EARLY. **That trade is gone**, the row now carries the extension scoped to the request limb, and the note is rewritten -- leaving it would have made it a lie.
  **THE FIRST INSTRUMENT WAS WRONG AND THE SUITE RECORDS IT.** A probe comparing dates with and without the method reported Hawaii unaffected. **It was a collision**: 45 days from 1 July lands on Saturday 15 August and `add_to_period_then_roll` gives Monday 17 August either way. Every assertion now reads `service_extension.state`, which arithmetic cannot fool.
  **TWO PROCESS NOTES WORTH KEEPING. (1) The insertion script's first pass put the new key's comma on its own line and, fixing that, silently removed a PRE-EXISTING stray blank line inside 15 of the 84 `service_extension` objects platform-wide.** Removing 15 of 84 would have left the seeds inconsistent in a way nobody asked for, so **they were restored and the diff is purely additive** -- the artefact count is HEAD 84 -> now 84. **(2) `sairn_load_state_check.py` caught that `version` had not moved on the 16 changed rows**, which is its whole point: a content change invisible to anything reading `version`. None of the 16 carried one, and live defaults absent to 1, so each is now `version: 2`. **153 of 423 rows still carry no `version` at all** -- not swept, because a row that never changed legitimately has none, and a partial sweep is the mistake avoided in (1).
  **ORDER OF OPERATIONS, DELIBERATE:** loaded BEFORE pushing. The old deployed engine ignores an unknown field, so the window between load and deploy behaved exactly as it did before -- no window where anything was worse than the status quo. Deploy-first would have made all 16 rows refuse `refused_undeclared_limb_scope` for a few minutes: safe, but a visible coverage gap for no reason.

LIVE against `sairn.vercel.app`: 16 of 16 correct in BOTH directions -- `not_applicable_to_governing_limb`/+0 on the summons limb, `applied`/+3 (+2 for Hawaii) on the paper-service limb -- and Delaware's and Montana's single-trigger rows still return 2026-07-06 `applied` +3, unchanged.** 2026-09-02

  **WHY THIS IS NOT AN ATS.** The audit records A5 as **baseline in this vertical** rather than an enterprise extra -- native in both AxisCare and Aaniie at Tier A -- and names caregiver turnover as *"the market's defining operational problem"*. **A bolt-on ATS can count applications. It cannot say which sources produce caregivers who are still working three months later**, because that needs the applicant joined to the caregiver joined to the visit history and **only the system of record holds all three**. Sources are therefore ranked by **RETAINED HIRES** -- not applications, not hires. A board that sends fifty people and produces one caregiver who leaves in a month is not better than word of mouth that sends four and produces two who stay, and ranking by volume says it is. **Asserted directly, both ways.**
  **RETENTION IS EVIDENCED, NOT INFERRED: a COMPLETED visit dated on or after the ninety-day mark.** Four outcomes and **three of them are about not knowing yet** -- `retained`, `lapsed`, **`too_soon`** (today is before the mark: NOT a failure, excluded from the denominator, reported separately, because counting it would make a source look worse the more recently it was used), and **`unlinked`** (hired with no caregiver record or no hire date -- reported, **never matched by name**, since two caregivers can share one and a wrong join credits someone else's work to the wrong source).
  **EVERY RATE IS OVER A DECIDED DENOMINATOR AND AN EMPTY ONE IS NULL -- never 0, never 100.** That is the discipline the referral panel and my own appeal-outcome breakdown in this same app already use. **Three panels computing "rate" three different ways is how a number stops meaning anything**, which is the real argument for reusing it rather than tidiness.
  **THE KNOWN LIMIT IS TOLD TO THE USER, not left in a comment:** this measures completed WORK, not payroll status, so a caregiver still employed whose visits were never marked completed reads as **lapsed**.
  **COMPUTED IN-PAGE, WHICH IS A REVERSAL OF MY PREVIOUS BUILD AND DELIBERATE.** I wrote `api/_lib/senior-applicants.js` first and **deleted it**: SAIRNsenior is local-first with a server hydrate, and its referral and training panels compute in-page. Moving one panel's arithmetic to the server would have made three panels in one app disagree about where a rate lives. **The drift risk that justified a server module for SAIRNdental is answered here by EXTRACTION instead** -- the suite pulls every function verbatim out of `sairnsenior.html`, so there is no second copy to drift from.

Tightened to assert the refusal site uses the map and that no ternary survives there; the break now fails 1.

  **VERIFIED ABSENT AT CLAIM TIME:** `location_id` 0, `sen_branches` 0, `region` 0 -- and the four `branch` hits in the file were **all comments about server code branches**, which is exactly the kind of grep that reads as a hit and is not one.
  **THE STATE IS THE POINT, NOT THE ADDRESS, and this is the part that makes B1 more than a label.** EVV aggregators, Medicaid programmes and caregiver training-hour requirements are **all set per state**, and this app holds a state in exactly one place: `sen_settings.evv_config.state`, **a single agency-wide value**. A two-state agency therefore has one EVV state and **it is wrong for one of them**. Recording state on the branch is what makes that fixable.
  **AND IT IS NOT YET WIRED TO EITHER, WHICH THE PANEL SAYS OUT LOUD.** This pass captures and reports the state; the EVV and training selections still read the agency-wide setting. **Leaving that unsaid would let a two-state agency read the column as enforced**, which is the more expensive mistake than not having it. Rewiring is a separate change with its own verification.
  **UNASSIGNED IS ALWAYS ITS OWN BUCKET.** Every pre-existing client and caregiver has no branch. Folding them into the first office, or dropping them from a total, **is how a rollup silently lies** -- so the columns are asserted to add up to the agency total **and** each bucket is asserted to hold exactly its own rows.
  **A VISIT AND A CLAIM INHERIT THEIR BRANCH FROM THE CLIENT rather than storing their own.** Stamping the branch onto every visit at creation would **freeze** it: moving a client to another office would leave their history attributed to the old one forever, and correcting it would mean rewriting historical rows. **Asserted by moving a client and re-running** -- the history moves with them and the agency total does not budge.
  **NO COST, NO MARGIN, NO PROFIT, AND THAT IS A REFUSAL RATHER THAN AN OVERSIGHT.** The audit's **B5 asks for per-branch P&L**. This app holds no payroll, no overhead and no cost of care, so a profit figure would be **an invented number on a screen a Tier B buyer decides from**. Revenue and hours are real and reported; **the missing half is stated on the panel rather than filled in**, and a test asserts the rollup computes no such figure.
  **THE TWO-LETTER STATE IS REFUSED IN THE PAGE AND ON THE SERVER.** Client-side alone is a suggestion; server-side alone lets the page look like it saved. Read is open to any signed-in employee (**a caregiver's roster row names a branch and the name has to resolve or the screen shows a raw id**); write is management-only. **No delete grant** -- a closed office is `active:false` and keeps its history, because deleting it would orphan every client and caregiver ever assigned to it.
  **⚠ THE EIGHTH NEGATIVE CONTROL EXPOSED A GAP IN MY OWN SUITE, THE SECOND TIME TODAY.** Attributing every unassigned CLIENT to the first branch **passed 28/28**: the totals still added up, and an Unassigned row still existed **because the unassigned CAREGIVER made one**. **Totals adding up is not the same as rows landing in the right bucket.** Each bucket's contents are now asserted directly and the break fails 1. Same class as the setup-file map assertion an hour earlier -- both found by breaking the thing on purpose, neither by reading the test.
  **AND ONE ASSERTION FAILED ON ITS OWN SUBJECT MATTER.** The no-margin check was a regex over a window of the file; it matched from `brRollup`'s **call site** straight into the panel's disclosure, which contains the words *"no margin or profit figure is computed here"*. **The test failed on the sentence that exists to say the thing it was checking for.** Now asserted against the extracted function body. Third time today a prose-matching assertion has tripped on its own explanation (after the "delete" grant check and the Mississippi carve-out) -- **the pattern is: assert on extracted code, not on a window of a file that also contains commentary about the code.**
  **⚠ ACTION REQUIRED: run `sql/sairnsenior_branches_schema.sql` in Supabase.** One new table, `create table if not exists`, safe to re-run, no delete grant. Until it runs the branch write refuses with `NOT_PROVISIONED` naming that exact file; the panel otherwise works from local storage.
  **VERIFIED: 29/29, 77 test files 0 failing after rebase, sairnsenior.html 1/1 script block, 218 top-level globals 0 duplicates, divs 693/693, 0 DOM targets referenced but absent, CRLF preserved, 0 stray CRs, seam check 39 clean / 0 not-forwarded / 0 could-not-tell. Negative control, eight breaks, files restored byte-identical: dropping unassigned visits fails 3, counting draft claims as revenue fails 1, counting scheduled visits as delivered fails 1, relabelling an unknown branch as Unassigned fails 2, accepting a free-text state on the server fails 1, letting any employee write a branch fails 1, dropping the not-yet-enforced disclosure fails 1, and folding unassigned clients into the first branch fails 1 after the fix.** 2026-09-02

- sairnsenior.html + api/_lib/sairnsenior-offline-evv.test.js (Hank, 2026-09-02) -- **COMPETITIVE-GAP ITEM A2 BUILT AND LIVE (`6ff2bc8`): AN OFFLINE CLOCK-IN IS KEPT, WITH THE TIME IT REALLY HAPPENED. 26/26, seven negative controls plus an eighth that hung.**
  **THE GAP WAS IN THE CODE, NOT IN A MISSING FEATURE LIST, which is why a grep for "offline" finding 0 understated it.** The existing clock path did `if(!saved){toast('Could not clock in -- check your connection and try again');return;}` -- **the event was DISCARDED**. A caregiver in a basement, a rural home or a concrete stairwell could not record the visit at all, and **EVV is federally mandated**, so a visit that really happened had no record and the agency could not bill it.
  **THE ONE RULE EVERYTHING ELSE FOLLOWS: THE RECORDED TIME IS THE MOMENT THE CAREGIVER CLOCKED, NEVER THE MOMENT IT SYNCED.** The queued payload carries its own `clock_in_at`/`clock_out_at` and is **replayed unchanged**; the sync time is recorded separately as `evv_synced_at`. **Re-stamping on flush would produce an EVV record that is precise, plausible and false** -- worse than a missing one, because nothing downstream could tell.
  **FIFO, STOPPING AT THE FIRST FAILURE, AND THAT IS NOT A STYLE CHOICE.** A visit clocked in and then out while offline queues **two entries for one visit**. Flushing in parallel, or skipping a failure to try the next, can apply the **clock-out before the clock-in** -- a completed visit with no start time, which reads as an EVV exception that never happened. Asserted directly: replay order is in-then-out, and a mid-queue failure leaves the remainder waiting rather than reordered.
  **GPS STILL WORKS OFFLINE and that is worth stating, because "offline" invites the wrong assumption.** `navigator.geolocation` reads the device's own receiver and needs no network, so an offline clock-in normally still carries a real position. **Offline is not an excuse for a missing location and is not recorded as one.**
  **NOTHING IS SILENTLY DROPPED.** The queue is capped at 200; at the cap the event is **REFUSED** and the caregiver is told the number and what to do, rather than the oldest entry being evicted. **Evicting would lose the earliest visit -- the one most likely to be already billed.**
  **AND IT IS VISIBLE, because a record sitting on one phone is not yet with the agency:** a pending count in the panel header, an *"On this device only"* marker on the row itself, a toast that says the time will not change, and a *"Sent -- recorded offline"* marker afterwards so a compliance reviewer can still see which records were captured without a connection. **The old code's success toast for a write that had not happened is exactly the silent-success class this platform keeps finding.**
  **THE `online` EVENT IS TREATED AS A HINT, NOT PROOF** -- it fires on a captive-portal wifi that routes nowhere -- so the flush stops on failure and simply tries again on the next trigger (panel open, or the next successful online clock).
  **NO TABLE, NO SCHEMA FILE, NO MIGRATION.** The queue is localStorage-only by definition: it holds what could not reach the server.
  **THE EIGHTH NEGATIVE CONTROL IS RECORDED WITHOUT A NUMBER BECAUSE OF WHAT IT DID.** Rotating a failed entry to the BACK of the queue instead of stopping **hung the suite in an infinite loop** and stalled the run for two minutes with the file left broken on disk. Worth knowing about the shape itself: **rotate-on-failure does not merely reorder EVV events, it spins forever offline.** The harness now runs each break under a 20s timeout so a hang is reported rather than stalling, and the file is restored in a `finally` so a hang cannot leave it modified. **That second part is the real lesson -- my previous harnesses restored the file only on the normal path.**
  **VERIFIED: 26/26, 79 test files 0 failing after rebase, sairnsenior.html 1/1 script block, 226 top-level globals 0 duplicates, divs 696/696, 0 DOM targets referenced but absent, CRLF preserved, 0 stray CRs. Negative control, seven breaks, file restored byte-identical: re-stamping the time on flush fails 2, discarding the event fails 9, evicting the oldest at the cap fails 3, dropping the offline wording fails 1, dropping the on-device row marker fails 1, not flagging the record offline fails 2, dropping a failed entry to carry on fails 1. Two of those crash rather than assert cleanly, stated as such rather than counted as clean assertions.** 2026-09-02
- sairnsenior.html + api/_resources/sairnsenior.js + api/sd-data.js + sql/sairnsenior_payer_contracts_schema.sql + api/_lib/sairnsenior-payer-contracts.test.js (Hank, 2026-09-02) -- **COMPETITIVE-GAP ITEM B4 BUILT: THE RATE COMES FROM THE CONTRACT IN FORCE ON THE SERVICE DATE. 61/61, sixteen negative controls. ⚠ NEEDS ONE SQL FILE RUN.**
  **THIS BUILD WAS INTERRUPTED MID-EDIT AND RESUMED IN A FRESH SESSION, AND THE INVENTORY IS THE INTERESTING PART.** The resolver, the API gate and the schema were all real and syntax-clean. **The entire panel and modal did not exist** -- no `panel-contracts`, no `pcmodal`, none of the fourteen `pc-*` inputs -- and `pcRender` is null-guarded on every one of them, so the sidebar button navigated to **a blank screen in total silence** rather than throwing. `missing_dom_target_check.py` reported **20 missing DOM targets**; it now reports 0. **A dead control that fails quietly is worse than one that crashes**, and nothing in the suite would have caught it, so fifteen assertions now check that each element the code reads actually exists and that something on screen opens the modal.
  **THE PROBLEM IN THE CODE IT REPLACES:** `generateClaim()` created every claim with a literal `rate:0` and the biller typed the rate in by hand, one claim at a time. **A rate typed from memory is how a multi-payer agency under-bills for months without noticing**, and nothing in the record afterwards said which rate was *supposed* to apply.
  **RESOLVED AGAINST THE SERVICE DATE, NEVER TODAY.** A claim is billed under the contract in force on the day the work was done. Resolving against today would **re-rate historical claims every time a contract renewed** and would silently change what an already-submitted claim says it was worth. Asserted by pricing a 2025 visit under a contract that has since been superseded.
  **SPECIFIC BEATS GENERAL, AND AN UNKNOWN STATE MATCHES NOTHING SCOPED.** A state-scoped contract outranks an agency-wide one for the same payer. The state comes from the **client's branch** (B1, shipped earlier tonight), so a client with no branch has **no known state** -- `''` means *not known*, never *no state*, and a state-scoped contract does not match them. **Applying it anyway would be assuming the state, which is the whole thing that column exists to stop.**
  **WHERE TWO EQUALLY SPECIFIC CONTRACTS ARE BOTH IN FORCE, NOTHING IS APPLIED.** Not the newer, not the higher, not the first row. **Billing at a rate nobody chose is worse than billing at zero, because a zero is visible on the screen and a plausible wrong rate is not.** The claim is created at a visible zero, the reason is **stored on the row** (`rate_source`, `rate_source_note`) rather than only shown in a toast that scrolls away, and the toast stays up for nine seconds instead of four.
  **AND THE OVERLAP IS SURFACED ON THE PANEL, NOT ONLY AT BILLING TIME.** A conflict found while creating a claim is found by a biller in a hurry; found on the Contracts panel it is found by whoever can actually fix it. The message names the fix (term one, or scope one to a state) and a test asserts **that fix is the fix that works**.
  **ONE DEFECT FOUND WHILE WRITING THE TESTS, NOT BY THEM.** `pcInForce` skipped a `term_on` that was present but unreadable, treating it as if the field were blank -- **turning a malformed end date into an open-ended one and pricing claims under an expired contract forever.** Now a present-but-unreadable term date makes the contract not in force, so the claim lands at a visible zero with a reason. The silent-wrong direction was the default.
  **`fmt()` NOW SHOWS CENTS, AND THAT TOUCHES FOUR CALL SITES THAT ARE NOT MINE.** It rounded to whole dollars, so a $28.50 contract rate read back as **"$28/hr"** and a $427.50 claim as **"$428"** -- a rounding of the display, not of the stored number, and not a figure anyone can reconcile against a remittance. Branch revenue, referral revenue and appeal recovery now carry cents too: noisier, and never wrong.
  **HYDRATION REPLACES RATHER THAN ONLY ADDING, WHICH IS THE OPPOSITE OF `senHydrateClaims()` DELIBERATELY.** A claim's local copy may be an unsynced edit and must not be clobbered. **A contract is the reverse:** a stale local row is a rate changed on another manager's machine that never arrived, which is precisely the billing-from-memory failure this panel exists to remove -- so the server row wins and local-only rows are kept. Billing hydrates contracts alongside claims, so a biller who goes straight to Billing on a fresh device does not price a screenful of claims at zero against an empty local list. Saving calls `pcPaint` and **not** `pcRender`, because a hydrate firing the instant before the write lands would read the row back as the server still has it and **silently undo the edit just made on screen**.
  **⚠ ACTION REQUIRED: run `sql/sairnsenior_payer_contracts_schema.sql` in Supabase.** One new table, `create table if not exists`, safe to re-run, **no delete grant** -- a retired contract gets a `term_on` and keeps its history, because deleting it would leave every claim it priced with no explanation of where the rate came from. Until it runs the write refuses with `NOT_PROVISIONED` naming that exact file; the panel otherwise works from local storage.
  **THE GATE IS MANAGEMENT-ONLY ON BOTH VERBS**, matching `sen_claims` rather than the branch gate beside it: the branch split read open because a caregiver's roster row names a branch and the name has to resolve, but **a contracted rate is financial data** and nothing on a caregiver's, coordinator's or scheduler's screen needs it.
  **ONE NEGATIVE CONTROL SILENTLY DID NOT APPLY ON THE FIRST RUN AND THAT IS WORTH MORE THAN THE FIFTEEN THAT WORKED.** The harness patches bytes; `sairnsenior.html` is CRLF and so is `api/sd-data.js`, and the management-gate break was written with a bare LF. It matched nothing. **A break that fails to apply is indistinguishable from a break the suite caught** -- the harness counts matches and reports `NOT-APPLIED` rather than calling `replace()` and trusting it, which is the only reason this was visible. Same class as scrubber item 16 shape B.
  **AND ONE BREAK CRASHED THE SUITE PARTWAY, UNDERCOUNTING ITSELF.** Resolving ambiguity to the newest row left `r.candidates` undefined and a bare `.map()` threw, taking the remaining assertions with it: the break reported **2 failures when it really caused 5**. Guarded, and the count is now honest.
  **VERIFIED: 61/61, 81 test files 0 failing, sairnsenior.html 1/1 script block `node --check` clean, 203 top-level globals 0 duplicates, divs 729/729, 0 DOM targets referenced but absent (was 20), 15 panels / 15 sidebar buttons, 0 dead buttons, 0 dead functions, CRLF preserved 3957/3957, 0 stray CRs. Negative control, sixteen breaks, both files restored byte-identical: resolving against today fails 1, taking the newest on ambiguity fails 5, matching a scoped contract to an unknown state fails 3, treating a malformed end date as open-ended fails 1, accepting a zero rate in the browser fails 1, shipping with no panel markup fails 1, no control opening the modal fails 1, rounding money to whole dollars fails 1, add-only hydration fails 1, Billing not hydrating contracts fails 1, saving via pcRender fails 1, dropping the stored reason fails 1, opening the read gate to any employee fails 1, accepting a zero rate on the server fails 1, accepting a free-text state fails 1, and reporting success against an unprovisioned table fails 1.**
  **FOLLOW-UP, NOT FIXED HERE AND DELIBERATELY NOT RIDDEN ALONG:** `sen_branches` (B1, tonight) has the **identical hydration gap** -- it is written to the server and never read back, so a branch created on one manager's machine is invisible on another's, and `brName()` renders *"(branch not on this device)"* for it. Same shape as the contracts gap fixed above; it needs its own change and its own verification. 2026-09-02

  **THE AUDIT'S EVIDENCE UNDERSTATED WHAT WAS ALREADY THERE, AND CHECKING FIRST CHANGED THE BUILD.** A3 is recorded as *"'authorization' 8 hits; 'units' zero. No burn-down."* -- but `senAuthRows()` / `rAuthBurnDown()` **already existed** on the Clients panel, headed *"Authorization Burn-Down -- this week"*. It is real and correct and it stays. What it measures is `sen_clients.authorized_hours`: **a single weekly hours figure with no number, no payer, no period, no expiry and no history.** Building the new panel without finding it would have shipped two screens both called "authorization" that legitimately disagree.
  **THE DISTINCTION IS THE WHOLE FEATURE.** A weekly care-plan figure answers *"is this week's schedule sane"*. A Medicaid or MCO authorisation is issued for a **period** -- 520 units between January and June -- and the agency is paid for what falls inside it. A weekly figure with no start and no end **cannot say how much of that period is left, cannot expire, and cannot be cited on a claim or in an appeal.** The old table is now headed **Care-Plan Hours** and says on screen that it is not the payer authorisation and where that lives; the new panel says which question it answers. A test asserts both.
  **CONSUMPTION IS COMPUTED FROM VISITS AND NEVER STORED.** There is no `units_used` column, no `units_used` field, and **the server deletes one if a client posts it** -- otherwise it would be stored on the row and read back by the next device looking exactly like a figure the server computed. A stored counter drifts the moment a visit is edited, cancelled or re-clocked, and nothing afterwards can say whether the counter or the visits are right.
  **DELIVERED, SCHEDULED AND NEVER-CLOCKED ARE THREE SEPARATE NUMBERS, matching the discipline the weekly view already had.** Delivered counts only real clocked time. **Merging still-scheduled visits into it is how a screen reports room that has already been scheduled away.** A PAST visit nobody clocked counts toward **neither** and is reported as its own figure -- scheduling a visit is not evidence it happened.
  **REMAINING IS AGAINST DELIVERED ONLY, and that is a decision rather than an oversight.** Netting off still-scheduled visits would spend units before the work happened, and **a cancellation would then hand back units that were never spent** -- a number that moves for reasons which are not deliveries. Asserted by cancelling a booking and checking remaining does not budge while scheduled does.
  **UNITS ARE CONVERTED EXACTLY AND NO STATE ROUNDING RULE IS INVENTED.** Real Medicaid programmes round per visit and **the rule differs by state** -- some round down, some apply an eight-minute rule, some bill exact. Picking one would make every figure here quietly wrong wherever it does not apply, so **none is picked**, 1h50m at 15-minute units reads 7.33, and the panel says so where the number is shown. **A units figure that is confidently wrong is worse than an exact one labelled as unrounded.**
  **AMBIGUITY REFUSES, for a sharper reason than it does for a rate.** Two authorisations in force for one client on one date resolve to nothing -- not the newer, not the larger, not the first row -- because **burning units off an authorisation nobody chose makes the remaining figure wrong on BOTH records at once**, and neither says why. Marking one inactive resolves it, and a test asserts the fix the message names is the fix that works.
  **THE DANGEROUS CASE IS THE ONE NOTHING ELSE SURFACED: care delivered with no authorisation covering it.** Invisible until a denial arrives weeks later. Reported as a count over a **declared** 90-day window, and an **ambiguous** resolution counts as uncovered rather than covered -- treating it as covered would hide the overlap behind a clean number. A future BOOKING is deliberately not counted: that would make the figure a forecast rather than a finding.
  **AND "NO AUTHORISATIONS ON FILE" IS NAMED, NOT COUNTED.** On a fresh install every completed visit would otherwise be listed as uncovered -- hundreds of rows of noise that train people to ignore the warning. The panel says *"No authorisations are on file, so no visit can be checked against one. Nothing here is a clean bill of health."*
  **THE GATE IS SPLIT AND DELIBERATELY DIFFERENT FROM THE CONTRACT GATE BESIDE IT.** READ is management, coordinators **and schedulers**: remaining units are **scheduling capacity**, the person booking the next visit is exactly who needs to know there are none left, and an authorisation carries units rather than money so it does not leak what a payer pays -- which is the whole reason the contract gate is narrower. WRITE is management-only, and the **+ Authorisation button is hidden from a scheduler** rather than left to 403.
  **THE CLAIM NOW CARRIES THE AUTHORISATION THAT WAS IN FORCE THAT DAY**, resolved against the service date and **stored** (`auth_status`, `auth_number`, `auth_note`) -- an appeal filed six weeks later needs to cite it, and by then the authorisation may have been superseded. Where the B4 contract says prior authorisation is required, the toast now says **whether one actually applies**; before it said only that the payer requires one and left the biller to go and check, which on a screen where claims are generated one after another is the same as not saying it. An exhausted or overdrawn authorisation is called out at that moment, **worded "as of today"** because recomputing what was left on a past day would mean replaying every visit in order and a figure that looks like a historical balance but is not one is worse.
  **⚠ ACTION REQUIRED: run `sql/sairnsenior_authorizations_schema.sql` in Supabase.** One new table, `create table if not exists`, safe to re-run, **no delete grant** -- a finished authorisation is retired with `active:false` and keeps its history, because deleting it would leave every visit delivered under it with nothing saying it was authorised at all, which is exactly the record an appeal needs.
  **TWO ASSERTIONS OF MY OWN FAILED ON MY OWN PROSE, IN ONE FILE, BOTH SCRUBBER ITEM 16 SHAPE A.** `!/Authorization Burn-Down/` failed on **the two comments that exist to explain the rename**, and `!/units_used/` failed on **the comment saying there is no units_used**. Both re-asserted against extracted code and the rendered heading rather than a window of the file. Fourth and fifth instances of this shape recorded on this platform; the pattern holds exactly as item 16 states it.
  **AND THE B4 SUITE BROKE ON THIS BUILD, WHICH IS THE POINT OF HAVING IT.** Three of its assertions failed because the shared claim toast and the Billing hydrate call changed. **One was made STRONGER rather than relaxed to fit** -- it asserted the toast said *"This payer requires prior authorisation"*, and now asserts the claim says whether one actually applies. **One was testing the line rather than the rule**: it matched the exact two-element `Promise.all` literal and broke when an unrelated third hydrator was added, so it now matches on the elements.
  **VERIFIED: 83/83, 83 test files 0 failing, sairnsenior.html 1/1 script block `node --check` clean, 215 top-level globals 0 duplicates, divs balanced, 0 DOM targets referenced but absent, 16 panels / 16 sidebar buttons / 0 trapped panels, 0 dead buttons, 0 dead functions, reachability clean, CRLF preserved 4333/4333, 0 stray CRs. - sairnsenior.html + api/_resources/sairnsenior.js + api/sd-data.js + sql/sairnsenior_pay_rates_schema.sql + api/_lib/sairnsenior-pay-rates.test.js + api/_lib/sairnsenior-branches.test.js (Hank, 2026-09-02) -- **COMPETITIVE-GAP ITEM B5 BUILT: PER-BRANCH AND CONSOLIDATED GROSS MARGIN, FROM REAL WAGES. 76/76 + 30/30, twenty-eight negative controls. ⚠ NEEDS ONE SQL FILE RUN.**
  **THIS CLOSES A GAP THIS APP DECLARED ON ITSELF EIGHT HOURS EARLIER.** The Branches panel (B1, this morning) shipped printing *"No cost, overhead or payroll data is held by this app, so no margin or profit figure is computed here -- the revenue half is real and the other half is absent rather than estimated."* That was right with no cost data: a margin computed from nothing is an invented number on a screen a Tier B buyer decides from. `sen_pay_rates` is the missing half.
  **VERIFIED ABSENT WORD-BOUNDARY, NOT BY SUBSTRING:** `pay_rate` 0, `hourly_rate` 0, `wage` 0, `cost_of_care` 0, `labor_cost` 0, `gross_margin` 0. The 3 `payroll` and 2 `overhead` hits **were my own B1 disclosure saying the app holds none of them** -- exactly the shape that reads as a hit and is not one.
  **THE MIRROR IMAGE OF THE PAYER CONTRACT, ON PURPOSE.** A contract says what an hour EARNS; a pay rate says what an hour COSTS. Both effective-dated, both resolved against the **service date**, both **refuse** when two are equally in force. A visit worked in July stays costed at July's wage -- resolving against today would silently restate every historical margin the next time anyone got a rise, **including margins already reported to an owner or a lender.**
  **KEYED ON `employee_id`, BECAUSE THAT IS WHAT A VISIT ACTUALLY CARRIES.** `sen_visits` stores `assigned_employee_id` from the auth roster, not a `sen_caregivers` row id. Keying on the caregiver record would join to nothing -- and **a cost of zero from a failed join is indistinguishable from a cost of zero that is real.** The modal picks from the roster rather than accepting free text for the same reason. The negative control that re-keys it to `client_id` fails 12.
  **THE ONE THAT MATTERS MOST: A MISSING RATE IS COUNTED, NEVER TREATED AS FREE LABOUR.** Cost missing means cost understated means margin **overstated** -- so **the branch with the worst record-keeping would look like the most profitable one.** `visitLabourCost` returns a STATUS beside the number so a caller can tell *"this cost nothing"* from *"this cost is not known"*; collapsing those two is the whole failure. Uncosted visits are counted per branch, shown in their own column, and the margin carries a marker. **The direction of the error is known, so the panel says "overstated", not "uncertain"** -- a negative control that softens that wording fails 1.
  **AND THAT IS THE STATE EVERY EXISTING AGENCY IS IN THE MOMENT THIS SHIPS**, which is why the branches suite now asserts it directly: with no rates on file, all three visits are uncosted, margin equals revenue, and `margin_overstated` is true. That is the most flattering possible wrong number and the one most likely to be read as real.
  **BURDEN SITS ON THE RATE, NOT ON THE AGENCY.** Employer taxes, workers' comp and benefits differ by worker -- a W-2 aide carries them, a 1099 contractor does not -- so one agency-wide figure would be wrong for any agency running both. **Zero is allowed and means NOT MODELLED, which is not the same as none**, and the panel says the margin is overstated by whatever it really is where a W-2 rate is left at zero.
  **THIS IS GROSS MARGIN AND THE PANEL REFUSES THE WORD PROFIT.** Direct labour is the only cost: no rent, supervision, mileage, software, administration or insurance beyond the burden percentage. **The audit asks for per-branch P&L; this is the direct-labour half delivered honestly rather than the whole thing dishonestly.** A margin percentage over zero revenue renders `--`, not 0% and not 100% -- it is not a ratio at all.
  **THE MONEY COLUMNS ARE MANAGEMENT-ONLY, header and cells alike**, and the gate is **narrower than the authorisation gate shipped an hour earlier**: that one opened read to coordinators and schedulers because remaining units are scheduling capacity. **A wage is not.** It is the most sensitive record this app holds.
  **⚠ ACTION REQUIRED: run `sql/sairnsenior_pay_rates_schema.sql` in Supabase.** One new table, `create table if not exists`, safe to re-run, **no delete grant** -- a superseded wage gets a `term_on` and keeps its history, because deleting it would leave every visit costed under it with no explanation **and would silently change a margin that has already been reported.**
  **TWO OF MY OWN ASSERTIONS WERE PASSING FOR THE WRONG REASON, AND ONLY THE NEGATIVE CONTROLS FOUND THEM.** Both are scrubber item 16, and both are the **dangerous** direction. (1) `/rate_per_hour must be greater than zero/.test(api)` was matching **the sen_payer_contracts branch's identical message a few hundred lines above** -- the break that removed THIS branch's zero-wage guard scored a clean **76/76**. Now anchored to `prProblems.push(...)`. (2) `/Consolidated: '+fmt(out.totals.billed)/` matched the line **while it was wrapped in `if(false)`** -- existence where the requirement is use. Now anchored to statement position. The browser-side refusals had the same collision (`savePayerContract` carries the same guards with the same wording) and are now asserted against the **extracted `savePayRate` body**.
  **THE B1 SUITE'S TWO "NO MARGIN, NO COST" ASSERTIONS WERE SUPERSEDED AND REPLACED, NOT DELETED.** They protected a real property -- no invented number -- and that property has moved rather than gone: the rollup still invents no cost, every figure comes from a resolved rate or is counted as uncosted, and the panel still refuses "profit". Recorded in the file itself so a later reader does not read the change as a weakening.
  **VERIFIED: 76/76 + 30/30, 84 test files 0 failing, sairnsenior.html 1/1 script block `node --check` clean, 226 top-level globals 0 duplicates, divs balanced, 0 DOM targets referenced but absent, 17 panels / 17 sidebar buttons / 0 trapped, 0 dead buttons, 0 dead functions, reachability clean, CRLF preserved 4625/4625, 0 stray CRs. Negative control, twenty-eight breaks, both files restored byte-identical, every one caught: costing at today's wage fails 2, treating a missing rate as free labour fails 2+1, dropping the overstated flag fails 1, a 0% margin over zero revenue fails 1, taking the higher wage on ambiguity fails 5, dropping burden fails 1, a malformed end date read as open-ended fails 1, re-keying to the client fails 12, collapsing uncosted into applied fails 4+1, no panel markup fails 1, no control opening the modal fails 1, free-text caregiver id fails 1, calling it profit fails 3+1, softening the overstated wording fails 1, dropping the zero-burden disclosure fails 1, showing money columns to a coordinator fails 1, not marking an overstated row fails 1, Branches not hydrating rates fails 1, add-only hydration fails 1, saving via prRender fails 1, accepting a zero wage in the browser fails 1, any burden in the browser fails 1, dropping the consolidated line fails 1, a coordinator reading wages fails 1, a zero wage on the server fails 1, a 400% burden fails 1, rejecting an omitted burden fails 1, and reporting success against an unprovisioned table fails 1.**
  **ONE HARNESS IMPROVEMENT WORTH KEEPING: both suites now stub `senLocalToday`.** Without it the today-instead-of-service-date break threw ReferenceError and **crashed both suites**, which reports a break as caught while hiding how much it actually broke -- the same undercount as the unguarded `.map()` in the authorisation suite an hour earlier. It now fails 2 cleanly. 2026-09-02

  **WHAT IS ACTUALLY LEFT, SEPARATED FROM WHAT ONLY LOOKS LEFT.** Buildable in-house: StoneDesk GAP 2, GAP 7, GAP 8's remnant half; SAIRNsenior A2 telephony and B3 franchise royalty; SAIRNroofing B6 EDI; SAIRNdental B5 BI connectors. **Blocked on a vendor contract or a federal certification, where engineering is not the constraint:** SAIRNdental A1/A2/A3/A5/A6, SAIRNsenior A1/A4, StoneDesk GAP 4 and GAP 5. **Standing decisions that are not work items at all:** StoneDesk GAP 6 (QuickBooks, carried deliberately), SAIRNdental B4 (never recommended by the audit itself), SAIRNroofing A4/B2 (positioning findings), SAIRNroofing B3's certified payroll (refused because inventing a prevailing wage would put a fabricated number in a federal filing), SAIRNdental B2's reporting half (deferred to SAIRN-BACKLOG.md). **Separating those three categories is most of the value here** -- an item blocked on a DEA registration and an item nobody has built read identically in the audit.
  **THE AUDITS ARE NOT EDITED IN PLACE, ON PURPOSE.** Rewriting their state columns would lose the record of what was believed on the day. Each now carries a banner at the top pointing at the new file and saying explicitly that everything ELSE in it -- market analysis, vendor evidence, reasoning, premortem -- still stands and is still the reference.

Recorded in the new file rather than edited into CLAUDE.md, because that is a separate change with its own review.
  **AND THE MAINTENANCE RULE IS THE POINT:** the closing session updates the row **in the same commit as the build**, the same standard as a handoff not being written until it is committed. A status file that is updated afterwards decays into exactly the document it replaced. 2026-09-02
- sairnsenior.html + api/_resources/sairnsenior.js + api/sd-data.js + sql/sairnsenior_franchise_schema.sql + api/_lib/sairnsenior-franchise.test.js (Hank, 2026-09-02) -- **COMPETITIVE-GAP ITEM B3 BUILT: WHAT EACH FRANCHISE UNIT OWES, AND WHAT WAS LEFT OUT OF IT. 81/81, twenty-seven negative controls. ⚠ NEEDS ONE SQL FILE RUN.**
  **PICKED OFF THE RE-DERIVED LIST I HAD JUST COMMITTED, WHICH IS THE POINT OF HAVING WRITTEN IT.** B3 was one of only seven items across four apps that were genuinely open, unclaimed and not blocked on a vendor or a federal certification. Verified absent word-boundary before starting: `franchise` 0, `royalt` 0.
  **THE UNIT IS THE BRANCH, AND NO PARALLEL UNIT TABLE EXISTS.** A franchise unit is an office with its own territory, which is what `sen_branches` already models. Two copies of an office drift the moment either is edited, and every royalty would then be computed against whichever one the reader happened to open.
  **A DENIED CLAIM IS NOT REVENUE, AND THIS IS THE EXPENSIVE ONE.** The B1 branch rollup counts every non-draft claim as billed -- correct for an operational view, because it WAS billed. **It is wrong as a royalty base: charging a percentage of a claim the payer refused takes real cash from a franchisee for work nobody was paid for.** Denied and appeal-denied are excluded from every basis and printed on their own line. In the test world the royalty is charged on 1500, not on the 2600 the rollup calls billed and not on the 3500 total.
  **AND AN APPEALED CLAIM IS IN NEITHER BUCKET.** Billed, then denied, outcome undecided. Counting it overstates what is owed; **dropping it silently lets a franchisor under-collect and hands a franchisee a statement that does not reconcile.** It is its own IN DISPUTE figure so both sides can see it and agree what to do.
  **THE BASE IS DECLARED PER AGREEMENT AND THE SELECT OPENS BLANK.** Real agreements charge on gross BILLED or on COLLECTED revenue; the same claim is August revenue on one and September revenue on the other. **The difference is months of cash and it favours one side of the contract**, so there is no default in the browser or on the server -- guessing it would be picking a side of a contract this software has not read. A test asserts the same claim lands in two different periods depending only on the base.
  **A PAID CLAIM WITH NO PAYMENT DATE LANDS IN NO PERIOD AT ALL.** `blMarkPaid` now records `paid_date`; every claim marked paid before today has none. **They are not back-filled** -- assigning them to a month would invent when the money arrived and change what a unit owes. They are counted and reported instead, and a test asserts they stay out of a window as wide as 2020-2030.
  **THE AD FUND IS ITS OWN LINE.** Folding national advertising contributions into the royalty is exactly what franchisees dispute, so both are computed on the same declared base and printed separately.
  **A UNIT THAT CANNOT BE COMPUTED IS NAMED, NOT DROPPED**, and revenue from clients with no branch is reported on its own and **charged to nobody** -- attributing it would invoice a franchisee for work outside their territory. **Mixed bases across a network are flagged rather than summed silently**: adding a billed-basis unit to a collected-basis one is not a quantity that means anything, and the total is still printed because omitting it would hide the same problem.
  **ZERO ROYALTY IS ALLOWED AND IS NOT AN EMPTY FIELD.** A corporate-owned unit inside the same network legitimately pays none; refusing zero would force a fake percentage onto the one record that should read zero. Both percentages range-check 0..99.99 in the browser and again on the server.

  **THIS GAP WAS HALF-CLOSED, WHICH IS WHY IT WAS EASY TO MISS.** The public catalog shipped the same day (GAP 1) and publishes SLABS -- so the machinery existed and the audit item read as done from a distance. **`stonedesk-catalog.html` contained the word “remnant” ZERO times**, and nothing on any screen said the remnants were not in it. Found in the status re-derivation an hour earlier; it is the one verdict in that pass that moved to PARTIAL rather than to BUILT or OPEN.
  **REMNANTS WERE localStorage-ONLY, SO THERE WAS NOTHING TO PUBLISH.** The yard read and wrote `sd_remnant` (SINGULAR) in the browser with **no server table at all** -- a remnant existed on exactly one machine, and the public endpoint reads Supabase, not somebody's laptop. `sd_remnants` and a sync/hydrate pair are the actual prerequisite; the publishing was the easy half.
  **ONLY A PUBLISHED *AND STILL AVAILABLE* PIECE REACHES THE WEB, ENFORCED ON THE SERVER.** A catalog offering a piece that has already been sold is **the double-sale problem in miniature** -- the same failure the slab reservation compare-and-swap exists to stop, and the error iBlocky puts second on its own homepage. A Reserved or Sold remnant with the tick left on is dropped.
  **AND THE HELD-BACK STATE IS SHOWN, NOT JUST APPLIED.** The panel renders **three** states, not two: not published / **ticked, but held back — Sold** / on the catalog. A ticked box that silently produces nothing on the public page is the kind of thing a shop works out for itself by staring at a live page, so it is named on the screen where the tick is.
  **THE REMNANT PRICE *IS* PUBLISHED, AND THAT IS DELIBERATELY THE OPPOSITE OF THE SLAB RULE.** `publicSlabView` withholds a slab's cost, correctly -- that is what the SHOP PAID. **A remnant's `price` is the ASKING price** for a piece being cleared, and it is the entire reason a visitor would look. **Asserted in BOTH directions** so a later “consistency fix” fails loudly instead of quietly deleting the feature, and said out loud in the library, in the schema and on the shop's own panel.
  **`location` AND `age` ARE NOT PUBLISHED, AND THE SECOND ONE IS A REAL FIND.** The yard row maps the inside of the building. **`age` is a STORED DAY COUNT that nothing increments** -- `sdRemnantAdd` writes `age:0` and no code path ever adds to it. Internally it is the number somebody last wrote down; published, it is a fact that decays, and a piece shown as “12 days old” still says 12 a year later. Omitted rather than printed getting more wrong every day.
  **A MISSING sd_remnants TABLE MUST NOT TAKE THE SLAB CATALOG DOWN.** The remnant fetch is separate and **non-fatal**: the slab fetch still 502s the request, the remnant one yields an empty list and the section simply does not render. Failing a live public page to add a feature to it would be a worse outcome than the feature being absent, and a shop that has not run the migration is exactly the normal case on day one.
  **THE PAGE STOPPED CONTRADICTING ITSELF.** Its footer said flatly *“Prices are not listed here on purpose.”* With remnants on the page that is false, so it now distinguishes the two: slab prices depend on size, edge and install and are a quote; remnants are single offcuts sold as they are and carry an asking price. **A page that contradicts itself is worse than one that says nothing.** A null price renders as **“ask”**, never `$0` -- zero is the one wrong answer a price field can give.
  **ONE FILTER, ON THE SERVER.** The catalog page does not re-check status; a filter in two places is a filter that can disagree with itself. The public view is a **builder**, field by field, so a field added to the remnant blob later cannot leak by default -- asserted by adding `supplier_cost`, `internal_note` and `vendor` to a blob and checking none survives.
  **⚠ ACTION REQUIRED: run `sql/stonedesk_remnants_schema.sql` in Supabase.** One new table, `create table if not exists`, safe to re-run, **no delete grant** -- a sold remnant becomes status Sold and keeps its history, because it is the record of what the yard recovered on material already paid for. Until it runs, the remnant write refuses with `NOT_PROVISIONED` naming that file and the public catalog simply shows no remnant section.
  **THREE OF MY OWN ASSERTIONS WERE PASSING FOR THE WRONG REASON, ALL SCRUBBER ITEM 16, AND ONLY THE NEGATIVE CONTROLS FOUND THEM.** (1) A no-status-filter check matched **the comment saying so -- which WRAPS ACROSS TWO `//` LINES**, so the phrase does not exist as contiguous text; shape A, the wrapped-quote variant, now asserted on the extracted `renderRemnants` body. (2) The held-back label was matched as text, so disabling the branch with `false?` left the string in the file and scored a clean **50/50**; shape B. (3) The failed-sync warning was matched as text and **the same sentence exists in the SLAB toggle**, so changing `notify(!ok` to `notify(false` -- making a local-only save always report success -- also scored **50/50**; shape B again, and the harness itself first reported that break as NOT-APPLIED (2 matches) for the same collision.
