- docs/2026-09-02-competitive-gap-status-rederived.md (Hank, 2026-09-02) -- **GAP 2 WAS ALREADY BUILT. I WAS DISPATCHED TO BUILD IT, CLAIMED IT, AND FOUND THE WORK DONE -- BY MY OWN STATUS FILE BEING WRONG WITHIN TWO HOURS OF WRITING IT.** No code written. Claim released, nothing lost because the first step was reading the code rather than the row. **The row correction turned out not to be mine to make** -- another session had already fixed it upstream and my rebase collided with theirs, which is the maintenance rule working exactly as written. What I added on top is the staleness note and the coverage correction below.
  **THE MIS-READ IS WORTH MORE THAN THE MISS.** The re-derivation found `stonedesk.html:9074` saying *“It does not emit DXF or G-code to a specific CNC”* and recorded it as evidence of absence. **That comment is scoped and still true** -- it belongs to the saw-ticket half and says no MACHINE-SPECIFIC output, which remains correct and deliberate. A generic DXF exporter sits ninety lines below it. **That is the same shape as the four catches I was pleased about in that pass -- a comment read as a verdict -- run in the other direction.**
  **WHAT IS ACTUALLY THERE (Fourth, `ee51217`):** `nestBuildDXF` + `nestingExportDXF`, R12 ASCII, a button on `panel-nesting`, and `tests/nesting_dxf.js` passing **26/26** -- including a byte-identical-output determinism check and a Y-flip assertion for the very claim the code had previously asserted without a test behind it. **The G-code half is a stated REFUSAL, not an open item:** a DXF is geometry, G-code is a machine program, and emitting “generic G-code” would mean inventing feed rate, spindle speed, tool number, blade kerf and machine origin for a machine the app has never seen -- with a saw on the other end.
  **AND A COVERAGE-DISCLOSURE DEFECT IN MY OWN REPORTING, EVERY COMMIT TONIGHT.** I reported *“N test files 0 failing”* on six commits. **That N came from `api/_lib/*.test.js` and nothing else, and no commit said so.** The repo has **125 test files in four places** -- `api/_lib/*.test.js` (93), `tests/*.js` (10), `api/<app>/*.test.js`, `api/*.test.js`. `tests/nesting_dxf.js` is one my sweep never ran, which is part of why the row said OPEN. The figure was not false, it was **undisclosed-scope**, which is the coverage-disclosure standard this platform already has a rule for and I did not apply to myself.
  **THE REAL NUMBER IS 125 FILES, 2 FAILING**, and both are **pre-existing** -- verified failing at this session's first commit (`001c958`) in a detached worktree, so not a regression from tonight. `api/sd-data-appointments-photos.test.js` expects 400 `TOO_MANY_PHOTOS` and gets **401**; `api/sd-data-complaints-readonly.test.js` expects 400 `READ_ONLY_RESOURCE` and 200 and gets **401** both. Same shape: `api/sd-data.js` now rejects at the auth gate BEFORE reaching the branch each test exercises, so the expectations predate a tightening of that gate.
  **NOT FIXED, DELIBERATELY.** Whether the TEST or the GATE is wrong is a real judgment call about intended auth behaviour on SAIRNdental, and **guessing it in passing is how a security gate gets loosened to make a test go green.** Recorded so it is visible rather than invisible, with the correct sweep command written into the status file for anyone quoting a number.
  **ONE PROCESS SCRAPE WORTH RECORDING:** bisecting with `git stash` + `git checkout <c> -- file` in a loop left `.claude/claims/{cc,cody}.json` in an unmerged `UU` state -- other sessions' files, conflicted by my diagnostic. Cleaned by resetting both to `origin/main`; no commit of mine touched them and the two pre-existing stash entries were untouched. **A detached worktree is the right tool for “did this fail before my change”** and is what produced the actual answer. 2026-09-02
- tools/sairn_http.py + tools/deploy_verify_notify.py + four gate tools + tests/sairn_http_challenge.py + CLAUDE.md + docs/2026-09-02-competitive-gap-status-rederived.md (Hank, 2026-09-03) -- **THE 403 WAS THE SMALL HALF. THE DEPLOY WATCHER HAD BEEN SILENTLY NOT RUNNING ON EVERY PUSH AND SAYING NOTHING.**
  **THE FIX IS THE USER-AGENT, AND IT WAS MEASURED RATHER THAN ASSUMED.** While the challenge was active, same second, same URL: **python-urllib default UA -> 403 `X-Vercel-Mitigated: challenge`; browser UA -> 200, 2,384,655 bytes.** I had recommended routing every check through the Vercel MCP tool; that works but only from inside a Claude turn, not from a hook or a cron, and it is a heavier fix than the problem needs. **Correcting my own recommendation: the header is the fix, the MCP tool is the fallback for a live turn.**
  **THE PART THAT ACTUALLY COST SOMETHING:** `deploy_verify_notify.py` caught EVERY fetch exception and `sys.exit(0)` under the comment *“network hiccup -- stay silent rather than false-alarm”*. Right for a hiccup, wrong for a challenge. **During the window, the post-push deploy check did not run on any push and reported nothing -- indistinguishable from a clean pass.** A verification that quietly stops verifying is worse than none, because it is trusted. The challenge now has its own branch, exits 2, and says **“THE DEPLOY IS UNVERIFIED, which is not the same as verified-good.”**
  **ONE MODULE KNOWS THIS, NOT FIVE COPIES.** `tools/sairn_http.py` carries the UA, a typed `Challenged`, `with_browser_ua()` for callers that build their own Request, and `raise_if_challenge()` for callers that parse their own error bodies. Wired into the deploy watcher, `sairn_load_state_check.py`, `licence_recoverability_check.py`, `load_deadline_seed.py` and `sairn_app_map_check.py` -- **whose honest `User-Agent: sairn-app-map-check` was read as exactly what it said and 403'd every route, so `--live` looked like a total outage.** It returns real results again (2 drift rows, both pre-existing).
  **THE GATES MATTERED MOST.** CLAUDE.md already says the seed gate's could-not-tell **“is not a pass”**. A challenge parsed as an inconclusive reply turns *“I was blocked”* into *“I checked and could not tell”* -- two states the rule already distinguishes and the code could not. `raise_if_challenge` makes them different again.
  **26 OFFLINE ASSERTIONS, NO NETWORK.** A test that needs a live challenge to fail only works during an outage. Includes the opposite mistake: **a bare 403, a 403 with only a Vercel id, and a different mitigation value must NOT be reported as challenges** -- calling every 403 a challenge would hide a real permission failure behind a reassuring “not an outage” message. Also asserts no protection-bypass secret was smuggled in; deployment protection is a different mechanism and is OFF for this project.
  **TWO DOC CORRECTIONS ON CODY'S FINDING.** (1) **SAIRNsenior A2's telephony half is VENDOR-BLOCKED, not in-house** -- it needs a real inbound DID and an IVR provider to receive a caregiver's call and hand back caller-ID. **The mistake is instructive: the offline half genuinely WAS in-house, and the row inherited that judgement for the half sitting beside it in the same audit item.** A compound item can have halves on opposite sides of a vendor gate. With it moved, **SAIRNsenior has nothing left that is open and buildable in-house.** (2) **CLAUDE.md's claim-matcher defect is FIXED** (`3f9d50a`, Cody, `tests/claims/run_matcher_probe.py` 0 failures) -- the note said “neither fixed”. **The discipline is kept rather than deleted with it**, because SUBJECT-level false positives remain: `platform` and `accounting` still block on the namespace, which hit me twice. Naming the subject accurately (`live-verify-tooling`) is the honest fix and is **not** the same as rewording a task to evade the matcher -- the note now says to state which one you did.
  **VERIFIED: 26/26 challenge assertions, matcher probe 0 failures, 127 JS test files 2 failing (both pre-existing, `api/sd-data-*` expecting 400 and getting 401, verified at `001c958` in a detached worktree), all tools/ and tests/ Python parses clean, `licence_recoverability_check.py` and `sairn_app_map_check.py --live` both return real data through the new path.** 2026-09-03
- stonedesk.html + api/sd-data.js + api/_resources/stonedesk.js + sql/stonedesk_locations_schema.sql + tests/stonedesk_locations.js + docs status row (Hank, 2026-09-03) -- **COMPETITIVE-GAP GAP 7 BUILT: YARDS. 54/54, twenty-three negative controls. ⚠ NEEDS ONE SQL FILE RUN.**
  **THE LAST GENUINELY OPEN, IN-HOUSE ITEM ON THE RE-DERIVED LIST.** Verified absent word-boundary first: `location_id` 0, `locationId` 0, `sd_locations` 0, `multiLocation` 0, `yard_id` 0.
  **THE SLAB IS THE ONLY RECORD THAT CARRIES A LOCATION.** Quotes, jobs, POs and remnants derive theirs and store none of their own. **Stamping a yard onto a job at creation FREEZES it** -- move the work and the history stays with the old yard forever, and correcting it means rewriting historical rows. Same shape as SAIRNsenior B1's visits and claims deriving their branch from the client. A test names quote/job/po/remnant/invoice explicitly so a later session adding a stamp to any of them fails rather than quietly freezing that record.
  **`location_id` IS THE YARD; `yardLocation` IS THE BAY INSIDE IT.** The slab already carried `yardLocation` as free text (“Rack B, row 2”) from the Add Slab prompt, untouched. **That conflation is exactly why this gap could read as partly built** -- grepping `location` in a 2.4MB file returns plenty, none of it a business location, and the word-boundary count that mattered was zero.
  **ATTRIBUTION, NOT ACCESS CONTROL, AND THE PANEL SAYS SO IN THOSE WORDS.** A multi-yard shop usually also wants staff scoped to their own yard; that is an authorisation change reaching every panel, every read gate and the employee roster. **Shipping attribution while implying separation would be the more expensive half-truth** -- a shop would believe its yards were partitioned when every employee still sees every one.
  **UNASSIGNED IS ALWAYS ITS OWN ROW, AND THAT IS THE STATE EVERY SHOP IS IN ON DAY ONE** -- the field is new, so no slab has a yard. Folding those into the first yard would attribute real stone to a place it is not; dropping them would lose it entirely. It sorts LAST (a gap to close, not a yard to rank) and is **selectable in the filter**, because otherwise the slabs needing attention are the one group nobody can list.
  **THE ROLLUP IS COMPUTED FROM THE SLABS EVERY TIME, NEVER STORED**, and a slab with no cost is **counted as unpriced, never summed as $0** -- the rule the Slabs KPI row already follows. A yard this device does not hold reads “(yard not on this device)” rather than a raw id, and its slabs are **not** relabelled Unassigned: they were assigned, to something not here.
  **NO GUESS IS EVER MADE ON CREATION.** The yard prompt appears only when a second yard exists -- on a single-yard shop it would be a question with one answer. An out-of-range or unreadable reply leaves the slab **Unassigned rather than defaulting to the first yard**; guessing puts real stone in the wrong place on every screen that reads it.
  **⚠ ACTION REQUIRED: run `sql/stonedesk_locations_schema.sql` in Supabase.** One new table, safe to re-run, **no delete grant** -- a closed yard is `active:false` and keeps its history. **No sd_slabs migration is needed**: `location_id` lives in that table's existing jsonb blob, so an existing slab simply has no key, which is exactly the Unassigned state the rollup reports rather than a null needing a back-filled guess.
  **THREE OF MY OWN ASSERTIONS FAILED THEIR NEGATIVE CONTROLS, AND ONE WAS THE WORST KIND.** (1) **The harness REIMPLEMENTED `sdLocName` and `sdLocActive` as stubs instead of extracting them**, so the break that made the real `sdLocName` relabel an unknown yard “Unassigned” scored a clean **54/54** -- the test was asserting its own copy. Now extracted via a new `fnAssign()`. (2) The server's unnamed-yard refusal matched the message, which survives `if(false)` -- item 16 shape B, anchored to the guard. (3) One of my own BREAKS was a no-op that only added a comment, so it proved nothing; rewritten to actually cache the rollup.
  **AND TWO SHELL-QUOTING SCRAPES WORTH RECORDING:** writing JS through a bash heredoc mangled `
` into literal newlines inside string literals and **broke the whole script block**, and a byte-patch built with `
` did not match a CRLF file. Both caught by `node --check` on all 127 blocks. The fix that worked: **write the JS to a file with the editor, then splice it in by byte** -- no shell quoting anywhere in the path.
  **VERIFIED: 54/54, 129 JS test files 2 failing (both pre-existing), 127/127 script blocks `node --check` clean, 755 top-level globals 0 duplicates, divs balanced, missing DOM targets 162 before and 162 after, dead functions 59 before and after, dead buttons 2 before and after, nav check PASS at 64 sidebar buttons, CRLF preserved 0 stray CRs. Twenty-three negative controls, all three files restored byte-identical, every one caught.** 2026-09-03
- sql/sairnlaw_trusttx_cross_client_collision_2026-09-03.sql + api/sd-data.js + sql/sairnlaw_deposit_void_balance_guard.sql + api/_lib/sairnlaw-trusttx-collision.test.js + docs/SAIRN-OPEN-WORK-INDEX.md (Hank, 2026-09-03) -- **CRITICAL: A DISBURSEMENT FOR ONE CLIENT COULD RETURN ANOTHER CLIENT'S TRUST ROW, AND A FAILED ONE REPORTED SUCCESS. 27/27, fifteen negative controls. ⚠ NEEDS ONE SQL FILE RUN.**
  **THE THREE NAMED SAIRNLAW ITEMS WERE ALL ALREADY BUILT** -- verified in code, not from the doc: `panel-jury` with `JURY_PROTECTED_BASES` and an ENFORCED `PROTECTED_BASIS` refusal citing controlling authorities (Batson 12 hits, J.E.B. 3, and the panel says *“enforced in the tool, not just discouraged”*); `panel-mocktrial` with 11 `mt*` functions and LeMAJ decomposition; `api/_lib/intl-caselaw.js` at 12KB. **The queued list was stale the same way everything else tonight was.** The live list is `docs/SAIRN-OPEN-WORK-INDEX.md`.
  **ROOT CAUSE, ONE SENTENCE: the advisory lock is keyed on `(license_hash, client_id)`; the idempotency lookup and the unique constraint on `(license_hash, trusttx_id)`.** One per-client, one per-licence, and they disagree.
  **FAILURE 1 -- ANOTHER CLIENT'S TRUST ROW, RETURNED AS YOURS.** The retry lookup had no client predicate, so a disbursement for client B whose id already existed under client A took the retry branch and returned **A's row** -- amount, matter, description, reference number -- with HTTP 200 `{ok:true}`. No money moved for B and nothing said so.
  **FAILURE 2 -- A PHANTOM DISBURSEMENT THAT REPORTED SUCCESS.** `on conflict do nothing returning *` returns no row, so the function returned null, and the API did `data: row ? row.data : payload` -- **echoing the caller's own payload back as the stored row.** Screen said posted; ledger had nothing; the trust balance disagreed with itself in the client's favour on screen.
  **THE INDEX ROW NAMED ONLY THE SECOND ONE**, and called it “can return a null row”. The cross-client leak was the worse half and was not written down.
  **WHY THE LOCK DID NOT HELP:** two calls colliding on the id but naming different clients take **different** advisory locks and never serialize against each other.
  **REACHABLE, AND CHECKED RATHER THAN ASSUMED:** `newId()` is `Date.now()` plus `floor(random()*1000)` -- a thousand suffixes per millisecond, not a UUID, shared across every client in the firm. 1-in-1000 in the same millisecond, and the birthday bound makes it when, not if.
  **THE FIX REFUSES RATHER THAN REPAIRS.** Same-client retry still short-circuits to the stored row, unchanged. A same-id/different-client collision **raises**. It does **not** silently re-key on the caller's behalf either -- minting a new id inside the database would put a disbursement in the ledger under an id the firm's own records do not have, found at an audit rather than at the keyboard. The null path is closed; **no path returns null**. Neither error names the other client: the caller may know their id is taken, not who holds it. The lock stays per-client -- **make the rare case loud, not the common case slow**.
  **FIVE OF FIFTEEN NEGATIVE CONTROLS INITIALLY PASSED, AND THEY WERE THE FIVE THAT MATTERED.** Deleting the client predicate -- **restoring the actual leak** -- scored a clean **26/26**, because there are TWO client-scoped lookups and `.test()` was satisfied by the second. Same for the two raise sites, the two “NOT posted” messages, and two `if false then` breaks that left the constants in the file while disabling the branches. All now **counted** and asserted on live conditions rather than present strings. Item 16 shape B, found in my own test by the controls rather than by reading it.
  **AND TWO ASSERTIONS TRIPPED ON MY OWN PROSE BEFORE THAT** -- the SQL ordering check hit the comment explaining why the collision is checked first, and the API absence check hit the comment quoting the old line. Both now strip comments. Shape A, twice in one file.
  **THE SUPERSEDED-CHAIN TRAP IS DOCUMENTED IN BOTH DIRECTIONS.** Re-running `sairnlaw_deposit_void_balance_guard.sql` would silently restore the vulnerable function -- the same trap its own header already warned about for step 2. It now carries that warning about this file, and a test asserts it says what the restored version would **do**, not merely that it is old.
  **⚠ ACTION REQUIRED: run `sql/sairnlaw_trusttx_cross_client_collision_2026-09-03.sql`.** Create-or-replace only, no DDL, no data change, safe to re-run; carries a verify block and a reproduction.
  **VERIFIED: 27/27, 132 JS test files 2 failing (both pre-existing), `api/sd-data.js` node --check clean, the existing trust-void race suite still 8/8, fifteen negative controls with all three files restored byte-identical.** 2026-09-03
- sql/sairnlaw_trusttx_functions.sql + three gutted predecessors + api/_lib/sairnlaw-trusttx-collision.test.js + docs/SAIRN-OPEN-WORK-INDEX.md (Hank, 2026-09-03) -- **THE THREE-FILE CHAIN CONSOLIDATED. THE TRAP IS GONE BECAUSE THE DUPLICATE TEXT IS GONE, NOT BECAUSE A THIRD WARNING WAS ADDED. 36/36, fifteen negative controls.**
  **⚠ THE BATCH ITEM CHANGES: run `sql/sairnlaw_trusttx_functions.sql`, NOT the collision file I sent earlier.** The collision file is now an inert incident record and running it does nothing. The consolidated file contains everything it did, plus `law_client_balance` and `law_check_and_void_deposit` at their current definitions.
  **WHAT THE CHAIN ACTUALLY WAS.** Three files defined `law_check_and_insert_disbursement` with plain `create or replace`: step 2 (which also owns the table DDL), step 3a (which also owns the balance helper and the void guard), and my step 3b. **Re-running an older one FOR ITS OTHER CONTENTS silently reverted the function** -- no error, no warning, no diff. Step 2 needed re-running for its columns; step 3a for its two other functions. Either would have restored a disbursement function that hands one client's trust row to another client's request.
  **TWO OF THE THREE ALREADY CARRIED A PROSE WARNING ABOUT THE FILE BEFORE THEM.** That is the tell: the trap was known twice and answered twice with a comment. **A comment does not stop a `\i` in a SQL editor at midnight.** So the definitions were REMOVED from all three rather than a third warning added.
  **STEP 2 KEEPS ITS DDL** -- columns, constraints, index -- and is still the place to re-run them; it just no longer touches a function. **Step 3a becomes a pointer.** **Step 3b keeps its incident analysis and nothing executable**, because that analysis is why the consolidated file's refusals are worded as they are and deleting it would leave them looking like taste. A test asserts step 2 still has its DDL, so gutting it cannot have quietly taken that too.
  **ONE FINDING WHILE READING THE VOID GUARD, RECORDED RATHER THAN “FIXED”:** `law_check_and_void_deposit`'s lookups are keyed on `(license_hash, trusttx_id)` with **no client predicate, and that is correct** -- it identifies the row by id and LEARNS `client_id` from the stored row, accepting no caller-asserted client at all. The cross-client leak cannot arise there. **Said out loud in the consolidated file** because the lookups look superficially like the vulnerable ones, and a future reader “fixing them for consistency” would be adding a predicate on a value the function does not take.
  **THE TEST NOW ASSERTS THE STRUCTURE, NOT A WARNING:** each old file defines **zero** functions and points at the one that defines three; a fourth definer anywhere fails. Two new negative controls cover it -- re-introducing a duplicate definition into an old file, and gutting the pointer.
  **AND MY OWN TEST TRIPPED ON WRAPPED PROSE FOR THE THIRD TIME IN ONE FILE.** `CROSS-CLIENT TRUST-ACCOUNT LEAK` breaks across two `--` lines in the incident file's header, so the contiguous string does not exist. Re-anchored to phrases that do not wrap. Scrubber item 16 shape A, wrapped-quote variant.
  **VERIFIED: 36/36, 133 JS test files 2 failing (both pre-existing), the trust-void race suite still 8/8, exactly 3 function definitions across every `sql/sairnlaw_*.sql` and all 3 in the consolidated file, fifteen negative controls with all three files restored byte-identical.** 2026-09-03
- api/sairndental/public-book.js + api/sairndental/public-book-orphan.test.js + docs/SAIRN-OPEN-WORK-INDEX.md (Hank, 2026-09-03) -- **A FAILED BOOKING LEFT A PATIENT RECORD THE PRACTICE CANNOT DELETE. 9/9, six negative controls.** No SQL needed.
  **PUBLIC, UNAUTHENTICATED ENDPOINT.** The patient row was written BEFORE the appointment, so **every failed booking left an orphan** -- and the commonest failure is the **409 slot race two people hit on purpose** by clicking the same popular time. Anyone able to load the booking page could mint unbounded `dnt_patients` rows carrying name, DOB, phone and email. **AND THE PRACTICE CANNOT DELETE THEM** -- no delete grant, revoked platform-wide 2026-08-24. Permanent PHI debris in a dental record system.
  **CLEANUP WAS NEVER AVAILABLE AS A FIX** -- there is no delete to roll back with. So the ORDER is reversed: claim the slot first, write the patient once it is secured. A 409 or a 502 now leaves no trace at all.
  **A SECOND DEFECT THE OPEN-WORK ROW DID NOT NAME:** the patient write was a bare `await fetch(...)` whose result was never checked. On failure `patientId` still pointed at a row that did not exist and the appointment was written referencing a **phantom patient**, reported as a clean 200. Now checked, and the response carries `patient_record: 'saved' | 'not_saved'`.
  **THE BOOKING IS STILL REPORTED AS SUCCESSFUL WHEN ONLY THE PATIENT WRITE FAILS, AND THAT IS DELIBERATE.** The slot IS taken; telling the visitor it failed would send them to book a second time into a slot they already hold. What changes is that the appointment now carries the caller's **name and phone**, so the practice can act on it, and the failure is logged loudly with **both** ids.
  **THE 502-vs-409 HALF OF THAT ROW WAS ALREADY FIXED; THE ORPHAN WAS THE LIVE ONE.** Row corrected to say which half was real.
  **ALSO CLOSED WITHOUT WRITING CODE: the SAIRNlaw FRCP 6(d) row (⚠, unassigned).** It claimed `frcp-12a1Ai-answer-after-service` still grants a 3-day extension for service of PROCESS -- **a federal answer deadline computed LATE, which is how a default judgment is entered.** Fixed in `5d7330b`; `api/_lib/deadline-us-federal-service-extension.test.js` passes **17/17**, including the **mirror-image guard** (Rule 5 rows must KEEP their extension or the fix would report EARLY) and a standing invariant that fails any future row carrying an extension off a Rule 4 trigger. **Third stale row in this index tonight** -- verified by reading the code, not the row.
  **ONE NEGATIVE CONTROL PASSED FIRST TIME AND IT WAS THE ONE THAT MATTERED MOST.** Wrapping the phantom-patient log in `if (false)` scored clean, because I had asserted the behaviour and never the log. **The visitor is deliberately told the booking succeeded, so that log line is the only signal ops gets** -- silence there is the entire silent-failure class. Now asserted behaviourally, including that it names both ids.
  **VERIFIED: 9/9 new, the existing public-book suite still 7/7, 136 JS test files 2 failing (both pre-existing), six negative controls with the source restored byte-identical.** 2026-09-03
- sairndental.html + api/_lib/sairndental-storage-quota.test.js + docs/SAIRN-OPEN-WORK-INDEX.md (Hank, 2026-09-03) -- **A FULL DISK THREW THE SYNCED DATA AWAY AND THE APP SAID “REFRESHED FROM SERVER”. 33/33, thirteen negative controls.** No SQL needed.
  **SKILL NOTE, because the dispatch named one and it was not the fitting one.** `sairn-infra-debugger` **is present** (repo and user store, 119 lines) — confirmed before relying on it, as asked. But it covers Supabase 403s, Vercel env vars and “what is actually deployed”; this is a browser-storage silent failure. **The fitting skill is `sairn-silent-failure-sweep`**, which is also present and is what I loaded. Its rule 1 (*“for every save/sync: what does it show on failure, and does that path ever get exercised?”*) is exactly the question that found this.
  **THREE INDIVIDUALLY DEFENSIBLE LINES COMPOUNDING INTO A LIE.** `st()` was `try{localStorage.setItem(...)}catch(e){}` — **a bare empty catch**, so a `QuotaExceededError` was swallowed whole. `dntSyncFromServer()` then did `st(key,...);changed=true;` — **unconditionally** — so a failed write still triggered a full re-render **from `ld()`, which returns the OLD data**. And `failureCount` counted only NETWORK failures, so `dntRefreshPending()` reported **“Refreshed from server”**.
  **Real data pulled off the server, silently discarded, success claimed, stale screen.** A practice would be reading yesterday's appointment list believing it had just synced.
  **REACHABLE BY DESIGN, NOT BY ABUSE.** Appointments carry **base64 photos**, so `dnt_appointments_list` reaches the ~5MB origin quota first — and once it does, **every later `st()` in the same sweep fails the same silent way**. That is precisely the accumulation the open-work row predicted.
  **THE FIX.** `st()` returns whether it actually wrote and **always logs** when it did not — a write that vanishes must not vanish from the console too. The return is **additive**: the 44 callers that ignore it behave exactly as before, and it still never throws. `changed` is set only on a real write. Storage failures are collected **separately** from network ones.
  **BOTH CALLERS SPEAK, INCLUDING THE ONE NOBODY PRESSES.** The background sync in `init()` runs on every page load; without a report there, a full-storage device would show stale data all day with the truth only in the console. **A quiet failure nobody triggered is still a quiet failure.**
  **THE MESSAGE NAMES THE PHOTOS**, because “storage is full” without them is a dead end. It also says the screen may be out of date (the part that costs money) and that nothing on the server was lost (the part that stops a panic). **A non-quota failure is NOT blamed on the quota** — sending a practice to delete clinical photos for an unrelated error would be its own damage, and a test covers that direction.
  **THE TEST DRIVES THE REAL FUNCTIONS** against a fake localStorage that can be told to be full — `st`, `ld`, `dntIsQuotaError` and `dntStorageFailureMessage` all extracted, because **a stub of `st()` would be a stub of the thing under test**. Covers the quota case, a non-quota `SecurityError`, and an unserialisable value that throws before storage is even reached.
  **VERIFIED: 33/33, 140 JS test files 2 failing (both pre-existing), 1/1 script block `node --check` clean, 251 globals 0 duplicates, divs balanced, 0 missing DOM targets, 0 dead functions, dead-button flag 1 before and 1 after (D2 informational), CRLF preserved 5192/5192. Thirteen negative controls, source restored byte-identical, every one caught — including restoring the empty catch, setting `changed` unconditionally again, and letting the background sync fail quietly.** 2026-09-03

- tools/sairn_push_gate_hook.py + tools/sairn_load_state_check.py + tests/push_gate/refspec_and_override_probe.py (Hank, 2026-09-03) -- **THE SEED PUSH GATE DENIED THE WORKFLOW ITS OWN DOCUMENTATION PRESCRIBES, AND ITS ESCAPE HATCH DID NOT EXIST.** 38/38, nine negative controls. No SQL needed.
  **Both defects were found on 2026-09-01 by a previous Hank session and flagged rather than fixed; this closes them.** Neither was theorised -- the index rows carry the reproductions.
  **DEFECT A: the gate diffed `origin/main..HEAD` no matter what the push command said.** So `git push origin <sha>:main` was checked as if it pushed HEAD. An engine-only commit touching zero seed files was DENIED naming 13 New Hampshire rules that lived in a LATER seed commit the push was not sending. That ordering -- deploy the computation standard, then load, then push the seed -- is the one the gate's own header prescribes, so **the gate was refusing its own documented workflow.**
  **And check 1 read `sql/*.json` off DISK**, so even a correctly-scoped push was compared against whatever happened to be checked out rather than against what the push would ship.
  **DEFECT B: `SAIRN_SEED_GATE=off` was read from `os.environ` only.** That works in PREPUSH mode, where git runs the hook as a child of the shell. It **cannot** work in PRETOOLUSE mode: the hook runs inside Claude Code's process and inherits Claude Code's environment, not the environment of the command it is inspecting. An inline prefix set the variable in a child shell the hook never saw. **Eight deny messages told the reader to do exactly that thing that could not work.**
  **THE FIX.** `pushed_tip()` parses the refspec out of the command text (prepush mode uses the local sha git already supplies on stdin); `outgoing_files()` takes that tip instead of hardcoding HEAD; `export_sql_at()` writes `sql/` as of that tip to a temp dir and check 1 points the checker at it with a new `--sql-dir`. Pretooluse mode now reads the override out of the command TEXT.
  **THE OVERRIDE MATCHER IGNORES QUOTED MENTIONS, WHICH MATTERS MORE HERE THAN USUAL** -- this repo's commit messages and the hook file itself quote the literal string `SAIRN_SEED_GATE=off` in prose, so a naive substring match would have let `git commit -m "...SAIRN_SEED_GATE=off" && git push` silently disable the whole gate. A negative control covers exactly that command.
  **DEGRADED, NEVER WORSE.** If the export fails for any reason the working tree is used -- the pre-fix behaviour -- and the deny message now SAYS which reading it got. An unresolvable refspec falls back to HEAD. The old behaviour is still reachable; it is just no longer silent.
  **THE PROBE CAUGHT A REAL DEFECT IN MY OWN CHANGE BEFORE IT SHIPPED**, and it was the CRLF class again: `git show` returns blobs with LF, `core.autocrlf` leaves CRLF in the working tree, so the first divergence check reported **all 72 seed files as diverged on a clean checkout.** A note that fires on every push is a note nobody reads, and it would have buried the real divergence it exists to surface. Same false-alarm class as the skill-store mirror diff earlier the same session.
  **THE HARNESS ALSO PRODUCED THE FAILURE IT EXISTS TO CATCH:** the first negative-control script had no `finally`, a fixture typo aborted it mid-run, and a MUTATED hook was left on disk. Rewritten to restore unconditionally and to verify byte-identity at the end.
  **VERIFIED: 38/38 probe arms, nine negative controls all caught, source restored byte-identical, both tools `py_compile` clean.** The end-to-end arm reproduces the 2026-09-01 case in a throwaway git repo with a stub checker -- pushing the pre-seed commit no longer runs the seed check even with the seed sitting in HEAD -- and a stub records what the checker actually SAW, because the hook's atexit cleanup removes the exported dir the moment it exits. Prepush mode re-verified on real stdin for the normal, delete-only and override cases. 2026-09-03

- .gitattributes + docs/SAIRN-OPEN-WORK-INDEX.md (Hank, 2026-09-03) -- **THE LINE-ENDING CONVENTION IS NOW ENFORCED REPO-WIDE.** 4 blobs renormalised, fixpoint verified, zero content change. No SQL needed.
  **Closed on Michael's explicit approval** after a third instance of the same false-positive class in one session: the skill-store mirror diff (12 skills reported diverged, all identical), the push gate's own seed-divergence note (all 72 seed files reported diverged on a clean checkout), and the standing deploy-mismatch hook fires. Each looked like drift and was line endings.
  **THE ROW SAID IT NEEDED A QUIET WINDOW BECAUSE IT WOULD REWRITE BLOBS REPO-WIDE. IT DOES NOT.** Measured rather than assumed: of 1,436 tracked files only **159 blobs were CRLF**, and only **15** sat outside the two byte-exact archives. After the correct rules the real change is **4 files** -- `api/_lib/deadline-engine.js`, `sairngrounds.html`, `sairnscape.html`, `sql/sairngrounds_data_schema.sql`. Each verified byte-for-byte as the old blob with CRLF->LF and **nothing else**; `git diff --cached -w` shows zero non-whitespace change outside `.gitattributes` itself.
  **A `.gitattributes` ALREADY EXISTED and the row said it did not** -- that claim was true on 2026-08-24 and stale since. It governed three narrow paths and stated the repo-wide decision was open. Its rules are preserved; this extends rather than replaces.
  **TWO WRONG RULES WERE CAUGHT BY MEASURING BEFORE COMMITTING, and both would have done real damage.**
  **(1) `-text` on `archive/**` is the obvious rule and is BACKWARDS.** Those blobs are already pure LF while the working tree holds CRLF, so `-text` -- store the working tree verbatim -- would have **baked CRLF into 150+ preservation blobs**, rewriting the exact bytes the rule was meant to protect. `text eol=lf` leaves them untouched. Caught by inspecting the first `--renormalize` staging 192 files instead of the expected handful.
  **(2) `text eol=lf` on `docs/sources/**` NEVER REACHES A FIXPOINT.** Eleven captured statute files contain **CR CR LF** from the original HTML-to-text extraction, and git's clean filter is a single left-to-right pass, so a CR-CR-LF collapses to CR-LF -- still CRLF. **Proven by running `--renormalize` twice: pass one staged 15 files, pass two restaged 11 of them.** A rule that churns 127,000 lines on every future pass is worse than the non-determinism it replaces. They are frozen with `-text` instead, working tree re-checked-out first so nothing was baked in. Second pass now stages **zero**.
  **THE MANIFEST WAS CHECKED BEFORE DECIDING, NOT AFTER.** `docs/sources/sairnsenior/MANIFEST.json` says its sha256 is of the **original fetched bytes**, so the extracted .txt's line endings verify nothing -- and all **32** of its `text_chars` counts match the single-pass normalised length, none the as-stored length. That is what ruled out collapsing the double CRs as a content edit.
  **THE WORKING-TREE HALF IS NOT RETROACTIVE AND THE FILE SAYS SO.** Committing changes blobs only; files already on disk keep CRLF until git next writes them, and `git status` stays clean throughout because the clean filter converts on the way in. `git rm --cached -r -q . && git reset --hard` in a clone with nothing uncommitted makes a working tree match its blobs -- after which `.claude/skills/` is byte-identical to `~/.claude/skills/` and the 12 phantom diffs stop.
  **CLOSES THE `core.autocrlf` ROW TOO, and more strongly than that row proposed.** It asked for `core.autocrlf=false` per clone. `git check-attr text` now reports **zero** unspecified files, so no clone's config decides anything -- and unlike a per-clone setting it cannot drift back on a fresh clone.
  **VERIFIED: 4/4 blobs exact CRLF->LF with no CRLF remaining, `--renormalize` second pass stages 0 files, `node --check` clean on deadline-engine.js, 3/3 script blocks pass in sairngrounds.html and 5/5 in sairnscape.html (HTML-parser extraction, not `grep -c '<script'`), zero unspecified paths, pre-existing stashes untouched.** No session held any of the four files -- CC and Fourth had released sairnscape before this started, Cody's 'shared engine' is `accounting-connector.js`, not the deadline engine. 2026-09-03

- stonedesk.html + tests/intake_no_false_success.js + docs/SAIRN-OPEN-WORK-INDEX.md (Hank, 2026-09-03) -- **THE PROJECT INTAKE PANEL REPORTED SUCCESS IT DID NOT HAVE, IN FOUR PLACES AT ONCE.** 23/23, twelve negative controls. No SQL needed.
  **Every Supabase call in the panel was a bare `try{}catch(e){}`**, and `anon` has never been granted anything on `intake_submissions` -- 42501 permission denied, verified live with the real shipped key on 2026-08-26. So all four failed and none of them said so.
  **THE READ rendered localStorage as though it were server data**, one line below a comment reading *"Supabase is the real source of truth here"* -- backwards in practice. Same class as the Slabs SEED fallback in Guardian's lesson 6.
  **THE WORST ONE IS `intakeAccept()`.** It creates a Customer and a Job locally, marks the submission accepted, and the server update that makes that stick did nothing. The shop believes an intake is handled; the table still says pending, **so the same job can be quoted and scheduled twice from another device.** The toast said *"Customer + Job created from intake!"*
  `intakeDismiss()` deleted from this device only, so the row returns on the next successful refresh and reads as the app undoing the user. `intakeAnalyzePhoto()` threw away a **paid Claude call** without a word -- the photo goes back to Claude next time and the shop pays again.
  **AND THE EMPTY STATE WAS THE MOST MISLEADING THING ON THE PANEL.** *"No pending submissions yet. Share the intake link with your next client."* is what a shop saw when the read had FAILED. Empty and unfetched looked identical, and only one of them means there is nothing to do.
  **THE COPY BUTTON STILL LIED AFTER THE BANNER WAS ADDED.** CC's 2026-09-02 pass put a visible warning in the panel saying the form is not live -- good, and it does not reach the person who clicks Copy and is told *"Intake link copied!"* at the exact moment they are about to send a dead link to a paying customer's customer. The banner and the toast disagreed and **the toast is the one attached to the action.** Copy now names the 404; Share **asks first**, because unlike Copy it leaves the building in one tap and its message text asks the client to fill the form in.
  **WHAT I DELIBERATELY DID NOT DO.** No grant to `anon`, no routing through `api/sd-data.js`, no form. Those are the real repair and **both are blocked on things that are not mine**: the live table keys rows by a **plaintext `license_key` column** (22 columns in `db/schema_snapshot.json`, no `license_hash`, no `app_id`) -- the same credential-in-the-link design CC removed from the URL, so building against it would put the key back by another route -- and SAIRNdental's public-form pattern needs a `booking_slug` equivalent and a rate-limit table that StoneDesk does not have. Both are Supabase-editor migrations. **The row now says which half is closed rather than reading as done.**
  `INTAKE_FORM_LIVE` is a single flag; flipping it restores both buttons to plain behaviour, and a test fails if it is flipped while the URL is still a 404.
  **VERIFIED: 23/23 assertions, twelve negative controls all caught, stonedesk.html restored byte-identical, 122/122 script blocks `node --check` clean, 20/20 tests/*.js green, 123/125 .test.js green (the same 2 pre-existing failures, confirmed failing on a clean stash).** The test drives the REAL functions against a fake Supabase client that returns the real 42501 -- a stub of `intakeServerWrite()` would be a stub of the thing under test. **Its own first run caught a false positive in itself**: the bare-catch assertion matched the `try{...}catch(e){}` written in the new comment describing the defect, the same quoted-mention shape as the push-gate override matcher earlier tonight. 2026-09-03

- stonedesk-intake.html + api/stonedesk-public.js + sql/stonedesk_intake_photos_2026-09-03.sql + vercel.json + stonedesk.html + 2 test files (Hank, 2026-09-04) -- **THE INTAKE FORM EXISTS. THE MIGRATION THAT WAS ASKED FOR DOES NOT, BECAUSE IT WAS ALREADY BUILT.** 51 assertions across three files, 29 negative controls. SQL written, NOT run.
  **THE APPROVED PLAN WAS BASED ON A PREMISE THAT WAS ONE DAY STALE.** The brief: migrate `intake_submissions` to carry license_hash/app_id and a public slug, add a rate-limit table, copy the SAIRNdental template. **Checked before building rather than after** -- `sql/stonedesk_public_surface_schema.sql`, committed 2026-09-02, already ships `sd_public_shop` (unique indexed `shop_slug`, `published`, `license_hash`), `sd_quote_requests` ("inbound leads from the public form", land `pending`, deliberately NOT `sd_crm`) and `sd_public_rate_limits`. `api/_lib/stonedesk-public.js` already has `resolveShopSlug` and the rate limiter. **`api/stonedesk-public.js` is LIVE** -- probed: `{"action":"catalog","slug":"..."}` -> 404 NOT_FOUND, `{}` -> 400 naming both actions.
  **So the specced migration would have duplicated a day-old subsystem**: a second slug column, a second rate limiter, a second public endpoint, a second set of failure paths to keep honest. None of it was written.
  **`?shop=` MEANT TWO DIFFERENT THINGS IN ONE APP, AND THAT ALONE MADE THE LINK UNUSABLE.** `/stonedesk-catalog` has read `?shop=` as the **shop_slug** since 2026-09-02. `intakeBuildLink()` was writing `?shop=<company display name>`. A display name cannot identify a shop to the server, so **even once the page existed the link could not have submitted anything.** Now reads `sd_public_shop` through `sdData`. It does NOT borrow the catalog panel's `pcShop` -- that lives inside its own IIFE and is unreachable from this script block, so referencing it would have silently been `undefined` and shown every shop "no address set" forever.
  **ONE NEW TABLE, AND THE REASON IS A CHECK CONSTRAINT.** `sd_quote_requests.data` is capped at 64 KB (`sdqr_data_size`). A phone photo base64-encodes well past that, PostgREST answers 400, and the endpoint maps 400 to NOT_PROVISIONED -- **a customer would be told the shop cannot take requests because their photo was too big.** Photos go to `sd_quote_request_photos`, mirroring `sd_progress_photos` and reusing its 1.5 MB per-row cap. Per-ROW, not an array column, so a second photo cannot push the first over the limit.
  **PHOTOS ARE VALIDATED BEFORE THE REQUEST IS WRITTEN**, because rejecting one after would leave a request the shop can see with a photo the customer thinks they sent -- the same orphan shape as the SAIRNdental booking fixed the day before. **A failed photo write does NOT fail the submission** (throwing away a usable lead over an attachment) but is never silent: the endpoint returns `photos_sent` and `photos_saved` separately, logs the rejection with the reference, and the page tells the customer which photos did not attach.
  **THE PAGE VALIDATES THE SLUG BEFORE SHOWING THE FORM**, via the existing `catalog` action -- one call both resolves the shop's name and phone for the header and proves the link works. A form that renders and fails on submit wastes the customer's time and reads as the shop's fault.
  **NOT LIVE, AND THE GATE IS MECHANICAL RATHER THAN A REMINDER.** `INTAKE_FORM_LIVE` stays `false`. A test asserts `sd_quote_request_photos` is **ABSENT from `db/schema_snapshot.json`** -- when that assertion flips, the migration has actually been run and the flag can be reconsidered. Another test fails outright if the flag is flipped while the snapshot still lacks the table. **The page and route existing is not the same as the shop being able to receive what a customer sends.**
  **`intake_submissions` IS DELIBERATELY UNTOUCHED.** 22 columns, keys rows by a PLAINTEXT `license_key`, declared in no schema file. Nothing is built on it. Retiring it needs someone who can read the table to say whether anything in it must be carried across.
  **THREE THINGS THE HARNESS CAUGHT IN MY OWN WORK.** (1) An assertion matched its **own documentation** -- the migration header quotes the 64 KB cap to explain why photos need their own table, and the unstripped regex read that quotation as the constraint under test. Third instance of that shape today. (2) An assertion on a SUBSTRING of the action guard still passed when a negative control appended a third action to it; now anchored and counts the conditions. (3) A dead `_sdBizProfile.company_name` read left behind in the link builder -- removed, because a dead read next to a link builder is what a later reader wires back in.
  **AND ONE IN THE HARNESS ITSELF:** the earlier intake negative-control script converted fixtures to CRLF unconditionally. That was correct when written and became wrong the moment `.gitattributes` normalised the working tree -- every multi-line fixture silently became a FIXTURE-MISS rather than a failure. It now derives line endings from the file.
  **A CLAIM WAS WRITTEN BY HAND AND SAYS SO.** `sairn_claim.py` blocked on the single word **"public"** against cc's `sairndental public availability` work -- different app, different files, the documented SUBJECT-level false positive. Per CLAUDE.md I did not reword the task to slip past the matcher ("public" is still in it) and named the subject accurately instead (`stonedesk-intake-form`). The note in `.claude/claims/hank.json` records why, and records the REAL non-conflicting overlap: I am copying cc's dental public-form pattern, so their work is an input to mine.
  **ALSO CORRECTED:** adding a page moved the repo's HTML count and `api/sd-data-exec-context.test.js` caught it -- the CTO advisor's architecture line now reads "18 app files and 4 sub-pages". `stonedesk-intake.html` is a public customer form, the same category as the two `sairndental-*` pages, so the APP count is unchanged. **Noted and NOT changed:** `stonedesk-catalog.html` is a public sub-page by that same definition and is still counted as an app, which is why the total went 17 -> 18 on 2026-09-02. Flagged in the test rather than silently reclassified.
  **VERIFIED: 18/18 form assertions, 10/10 link assertions, 23/23 honesty assertions, 29 negative controls across two harnesses all caught, every source file restored byte-identical, 122/122 script blocks in stonedesk.html and 1/1 in stonedesk-intake.html `node --check` clean, 164/166 test files green (the same 2 pre-existing failures, confirmed on a clean stash), SQL preflight clean, credential guard clean, seam check clean, reachability clean on both pages.** 2026-09-04

- .claude/settings.json + docs/SAIRN-OPEN-WORK-INDEX.md (Hank, 2026-09-04) -- **THE GATES WERE RUNNING AN UNREVIEWED COPY OF THEMSELVES.** Repointed, proven live in-session. No SQL, no tests changed.
  **FOUND BY BEING DENIED BY MY OWN BUG.** Pushing the intake-form work, the push gate refused with the **pre-fix override wording** -- the text I had replaced hours earlier the same day. Eight of nine hook commands pointed at `C:/SAIRN/tools/`, a directory **in no clone and under no review**.
  **TWO OF SEVEN HAD ALREADY DRIFTED, and both mattered.** `sairn_push_gate_hook` repo 904L vs external 650L -- so the gate that actually ran was missing the refspec scoping AND the reachable override, i.e. **the fix for a gate defect was never in the gate.** `deploy_verify_notify` repo 233L vs external 189L -- the external copy is the **pre-2026-09-02 bare-urllib version whose `except` calls `sys.exit(0)` on any failure.** That is precisely the copy that trips Vercel's bot mitigation and then says nothing, so **every post-push deploy check since 2026-09-02 ran the version that cannot report a failure.**
  CLAUDE.md already says two copies of a gate is how one of them silently goes stale. This is that, with a measurement.
  **RELATIVE `tools/…`, NOT `${CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR}` AND NOT A SECOND ABSOLUTE PATH.** `.claude/settings.json` is committed and shared by all four clones, so a relative path resolves to whichever clone the session is in and needs no per-clone copy of the scripts. **`${CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR}` was MEASURED as empty in this environment** -- using it would have silently produced `C:/Program Files/Git/tools/...`, a wrong path that fails open. That measurement is the reason the old index row's suggested fix was not taken.
  **PROVEN LIVE, NOT ASSUMED.** All seven targets exist in `tools/`; each was pipe-tested with the stdin payload its own event delivers. Then the configured hook was proven firing **in this session**: a commit-and-push with SQL pending was denied, and the denial carried the **new** override wording, which only the repo copy contains. The five identical files are unchanged in behaviour; the two drifted ones now run the newer, fixed versions.
  **A NEAT DEMONSTRATION OF THE HOOK'S OWN DOCUMENTED LIMIT, seen by accident:** an earlier proof attempt created the probe SQL file and ran the push in ONE command, and was allowed -- because the hook runs BEFORE the command, so at hook time the file did not exist. That is the exact commit-and-push blind spot the hook's block comment describes, reproduced without meaning to.
  **FLAGGED, NOT FIXED:** `git_push_master_guard` denied a command whose only occurrence of "master" was the substring inside the filename `git_push_master_guard.py`. A false positive on a literal filename -- harmless here, worth its own pass.
  **VERIFIED: 8/8 commands repointed, 0 absolute paths remaining, settings.json parses, 7/7 targets present, 7/7 pipe-tested, hook proven firing with the repo copy's text, both previously-drifted files re-checked and now running the repo version.** 2026-09-04

Both terms are kept, because the day a management role is added that is NOT a broad reader, the redundant form silently changes meaning everywhere at once.

So it is guarded two other ways instead: a structural assertion that both terms are present in the source, and a subset assertion that fails the moment the two role sets diverge -- which is the moment every hand-written site needs re-reading.

Equivalence is instead proven **exhaustively over all 64 role x employee x row combinations**, which is stronger than two round trips for a pure substitution but is **not** the same as exercising the deployed endpoint, and the row now says so.

That corrects my own earlier note, which repeated an index row saying RF-AUDIT-2026 returned 401 -- it exists and works. **No exposed customer PIN was used**, which was the reason I declined the first time.

Reporting that as a green round trip would have been the exact shape this session has been fixing all night: a check that did not run, presented as a check that passed.
  **SO THE FIXTURE WAS CREATED RATHER THAN REPORTED AROUND.** `ZZ-GATE-JOB`, `ZZ-GATE-CLAIM` and a narrow-role employee `zz-gate-foreman`, on the AUDIT licence, deliberately labelled. **Not deleted afterwards** -- the platform has no reachable delete path for these resources, which is its own open row, so the honest thing is to name them clearly rather than pretend they can be cleaned up.

A foreman who is NOT the assignee is **excluded from the list** and gets **403 FORBIDDEN** on photos read, on reconcile, and on the **write path -- the seventh spelling I found by counting.** Then the claim is assigned to that foreman and **the same three calls pass.** That flip is what separates a working gate from one that simply refuses everyone, and a deny-only test would have looked identical.

My first probe sent `X-Session-Token` and every authenticated call answered **401 NO_SESSION** -- indistinguishable, from the outside, from a gate that rejects valid sessions. Found by reading `tokenFromRequest` rather than by assuming the product was broken.
